mr president, whereas in the past animals - except those which were traditionally trans-human - did not make long journeys and, indeed, during their lifetime, only made the journey to the slaughterhouse, the problem of transport regulation in its various aspects has today become an important component of the legislation concerning the livestock sector. the first need that has emerged is of a health nature, with reference in particular to the spread of communicable diseases. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the matter. the welfare of animals during transport is more directly concerned by categories of operators other than farmers, although the latter often suffer the consequences: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's farmers are able to obtain the necessary information and to obtain the necessary information. the regulations on the protection of animals during transport, and in particular the regulations we are about to vote on, are based on scientific and technical evidence. in this particular case, we recall the studies following the report of the scientific committee on animal health and welfare, which took account of more than 300 sources and produced 55 recommendations. if we look at the five freedoms mentioned in the bremmer report in 1995, we see that unloading, loading and handling operations have a profound impact on each of these freedoms. in fact, it is proven that this complex of operations causes the highest levels of stress and suffering in animals than all other breeding practices. the proposed time limit and the stopover system are therefore too restrictive and the reasons given in support of the shorter travel times are not justified from a scientific point of view: the committee itself did not indicate this limit in its report. transport can undoubtedly cause a whole range of negative situations for the animal, but it must be analysed in technical terms, both for the benefit of the animals themselves and for the operators.
mr president, i have always maintained that the degree of civilization of a people is also measured by the respect it has for animals. in this connection, i should like to thank mr maat for his excellent report which goes in this direction, for the improvements made in the committee on agriculture which, compared with the proposal presented by the european commission, help to give a more civilised image of our continent in the field of animal rights protection. but it is not just a question of image - of giving, that is, outside the borders of the european union, a more human image - but also, and i would say above all, of substance. the problem has, as has already been said, also health aspects. i would like to highlight a few of them. first, the maximum duration of road transport: given that animals should be moved as little as possible, it would be useful to limit transport to eight hours or a maximum of 500 km per day. this would apply to both animals for slaughter and those for fattening. however, for transport of animals for breeding, exhibition and competition, transport for more than 8 hours should be carried out by special vehicles. what does "special" mean? this means that these vehicles should be equipped with equipment capable of measuring the temperature and humidity in the animal compartment and should have a forced ventilation system that works when the vehicle is stationary. finally, in addition to the right provision for special training for drivers of vehicles intended for transport inadequate guidance, as is known, inevitably causes serious and unnecessary damage and suffering to animals i would like to stress the need for a public veterinarian to be present during loading of animals, to ensure that the rules of transport their fitness to travel, the respect of the minimum guaranteed spaces are respected. for this reason we support in particular amendments nos 121, 122, 123 and 124.
mr president, this is not the first time that this subject has provoked lively debates in this parliament and equally lively and painful decisions. our task is, once again, to try to achieve a genuine balance between the two opposing needs: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the transport costs are reduced, and that the transport costs are reduced, and that the transport costs are reduced. in short, the matter deserves all the attention, but in these difficult judgments it is comforting to note, however, the great progress made in technology, which today puts at the disposal of the transporters, of those who are diligent and diligent, increasingly comfortable vehicles, equipped with fans, feeders and drinkers with rehydrating water. these new instruments can make it possible to make trips more comfortable and, above all, longer, but still with stopping periods that can be extended by up to two hours. there is also a debate on the issue of unloading and reloading animals, an operation which does not always achieve its intended purpose of helping the animals to recover from their rest. there is a tendency on the part of farmers and transporters, but also environmentalists, to argue that this type of operation, especially for small animals - think of calves - could cause even greater stress than staying on board, especially on those vehicles i mentioned, equipped with refrigerated air, rehydration water and ad hoc feeders. this too is a proposal which should not be neglected, so as not to make mistakes or to repeat problems which have already been discussed. of course, you would have to be in the head or condition of a calf to be able to decide without making a mistake: but the calf does not think and, more importantly, does not vote.
in writing. - (it) the radical members of parliament voted neither for nor against mr lehne's report on the protection of the parliamentary immunity of marco pannella, because, although we do not question the position of the committee on legal affairs which the european parliament has adopted, we believe that it is necessary to stress the need for the privileges of parliament to be established vis-à-vis the member states in every appropriate forum. it is neither appropriate nor useful that the personal liberty of a member of the european parliament can be restricted - even if it is a judgment which has acquired legal force - without parliament itself being informed and thus possibly taking the necessary steps to make its position known on an assessment of the limits of its members' privileges. although the decision of the parliament seems to be correct in this case - also because the measures restricting mr pannella's personal liberty have been lifted - it must in no way be interpreted as a precedent should a similar case arise in the future.
there is no doubt - and it is trivial to say - that the middle east region is being torn apart by increasingly tragic conflicts. i really believe, and i do so without conforming, that we must also have the courage to say that a strong responsibility for unresolved conflicts lies with the international community and, in the first place, with president bush's military policy and those who, together with president bush, have spoken for that policy. we cannot avoid this problem and i believe, together with the president of the council and commissioner patten, that the palestinian and israeli questions are central, essential. i also think we need to get out of conformity and stop saying that the palestinians do not recognize the state of israel: they recognized it in 1988. is it not true that the palestinian authority does not want to deal with: she says she's willing to negotiate. the one who refuses to negotiate, who actually does other politics is sharon. i think we need to look at this objectively. there is no doubt that the palestinian authority bears responsibility for its failure to stop terrorist acts, but i also believe that we must assess clearly how much of sharon's policy has been a vehicle for the construction of terrorism. commissioner patten is right: this does not mean that terrorist acts in themselves, regardless of the policies that are implemented, should be condemned and suppressed. let's stop telling each other that all cats are gray. there are differences, and those differences are the prohibition and the failure to implement any international law for the palestinians. let us now think about this grand plan, or rather the working paper, that the united states has prepared for the g8 summit in june: it is an arrogant and imperialist project which, although it envisages the building of democracy, democratic processes, preambles, aid and support, does so without discussing it with anyone. we cannot export democracy - apart from that we should start thinking about our own democracies too - because democracy is a continuous process of which we are also deficient, in which we also sometimes have gaps and differences. the greater middle east plan, as it is put, is a plan that will inevitably not help the growth of democracy. it will probably help some accomplices, servants, but it will not help in the construction of arab countries which really need to free themselves from oppressive regimes, which need democracy. please, not with arrogance and imperialism! i think that the european union has made the right choice in opting for the long term and not demanding immediate results by cutting the gordian knot of ancient memory. so we need time to reflect and build relationships. the parliamentary forum in athens is important in this respect: we also saw the differences and the richness, not just the weaknesses, of the arab world. i would like to return to sharon's policy for a moment. in 1973 winston churchill's son asked sharon, "what is the difference between a man and a woman?" what do you think you're doing to the palestinians? he said: don't worry: palestinians can be a pastrami inside the sandwich. we'll crush them inside a sandwich. and that's what he's doing by erecting the wall and preventing freedom of movement. yassin's murder is not the first: 250 extrajudicial executions were carried out and the first victim was tabet tabet, an al fatah peace activist who worked with peace now. but why do we have to repeat these things over and over again? i'm tired of repeating myself sometimes and feeling like everyone has the same responsibilities. the responsibilities are different and i also think that, with this choice, sharon not only killed a leader who - out of charity! - as a woman i consider the height of negativity, but actually knew and wanted to turn a national struggle into a religious struggle. that's the drama! four days ago i was in the mountains of kurdistan and i saw demonstrations in van for sheikh yassin. that is the risk we are taking, that is what president bush has done. finally, i also agree with the proposals made by mr barón crespo on article 2 and the other points.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i believe that what is happening in the middle east is very different from what we could have been assessing a few days ago. in fact, i believe that the middle east is coming closer and closer, right into our own home. we see this in speeches with which i could not agree less in certain cases, such as that of mr morgantini, but not only: i believe that the terrorist attacks we are witnessing in europe must be assessed. we cannot suddenly consider only the problem of yassin's murder. i, as a catholic, reject any possibility, one day, of touching a person: not to kill her, but just to touch her. but that's not the point, in my opinion. i believe that if we are to guarantee our citizens peace, tranquility and serenity tomorrow, we must formulate a very different proposal and be guided by very different reasoning. europe as such cannot be the sole voice of a message in terms of european funding for the middle east. europe must put forward a real, concrete proposal. i believe that this can be done, and in this case i call on commissioner patten and the irish presidency of the council to call for a halt to the fighting. i believe that this can give politics a real voice and not just a subject to speculate about in this house. for someone like me who experienced terrorism - a different kind of terrorism, the red one - and saw a bomb inside the house and a brother go out the window, the political words at that time, in the 1980s, meant nothing. the response we got -- me as a young man at the time -- was unique and truly incredible. there was a unity of purpose in pursuing a single objective: the fight against terrorism. i believe that this can only be done if we as europe realise this and at least, at this moment, compile a list of terrorist associations to be evaluated in the international context. everything else, i think, can be considered just talk.
mr president, mr president of the council, mr commissioner, the european union has thus found its unison again by condemning the killing of sheikh yassin, and some have even gone so far as to present him as a kindly old man and a person with a smell of almost sanctity. today we heard in this parliament the authoritative president barón crespo and others, in my opinion, less authoritative, mention the possibility of calling for the application of article 2, that is to say the suspension of the association agreement with israel, because of the death of sheikh yassin, which is certainly a violation of international law: there's no doubt about that. the european council has devoted five paragraphs to condemning israel for this, but i do not think i have read anything in the text about the hiring of handicapped children for a few euros to be turned into human bombs with ten kilos of explosives. we radicals have called for the suspension of the association agreements - commissioner patten knows this very well - by invoking article 2, which unfortunately we have never, or almost never, put into practice for dozens of countries, many of them in the region. i think it's important to keep in mind this element: if we think it is right to condemn israel, a democratic country - by giving it, say, a license as a democratic country - for which we can demand respect for international law, but on the assumption that it is useless to ask, for example, other countries in the arab world to do the same, we are making a big mistake and we are continuing to babble without having an effective role in the region. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but the idea itself, not the export of democracy, so much to ridicule and trivialize the idea, similar to what we tried to achieve in a certain way in vitro, long before the american proposal, with the famous sana'a conference on democracy and human rights in the middle east, is the winning idea: either we anchor political democracy or, like europe, we join ourselves, of course, but with a strong proposal in the coming months, consulting the arab countries, having an approach that seeks to involve them - not just at the governmental level but also at the civil society level - in a strong and big proposal to promote those societies, that democracy that is required by those societies to achieve development, as the undp has told us. i think this is the way to solve the israeli-palestinian issue or to include it in the overall solution. in conclusion, i welcome the fact that finally great leaders of the european left are recognizing, as we have been saying for many years, that the accession of israel and palestine to the european union is another important path to a lasting solution to the middle east question.
mr president, the most accurate and thorough analysis of the limits of the development of democracy in the arab world and its consequences for the development of those societies came from the first report of the united nations development programme, drawn up in 2002. the second report was recently published and it looks in depth at the lack of education and training, the serious limitations in cultural production, technology and research. the most significant fact is that the team working on this project is composed entirely -- i repeat, entirely -- of arab researchers. the strategy for the greater middle east, which the bush administration has drawn up, is largely inspired by those analyses, with a limitation which i would call capital, namely that of indicating changes and reforms irrespective of any relationship with the arab and islamic world. this approach risks feeding the idea of an alleged superiority of the so-called western civilization over the entire cultural and religious tradition of the arab world. this idea is at the heart of the feeling of humiliation, so widespread in these countries, that terrorist groups are speculating on, hoping to in turn fuel hatred towards the west. the second point i would like to make is about the relations between the bush administration and the sharon government. the arab world cannot tolerate the double standard: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the american administration, which is so inflexible as to have inflicted two wars on iraq and a decade of embargo, is not moving a finger today in relation to what, just yesterday in geneva, was described by the rapporteur of the un commission on human rights as a humanitarian catastrophe. the concern is that the method chosen by the us administration and its indifference to the middle east conflict risk having the opposite effect, namely that of giving even more credit, in the eyes of public opinion in those countries, to non-democratic governments and political classes responsible for the misuse of resources, widespread corruption, the lack of democracy and fundamental freedoms, starting with that of women, in short, for the crisis and decline of those societies. europe has a role and a responsibility in all this, but also a non-improvised history of relations in the mediterranean area, based on a different method, namely that of partnership. this strategy has recently been reinforced by the neighbourhood policy in response to the enlargement to the east. so the point today is not to question all this, but to open a relationship with the bush administration, with the arab league and in collaboration with the plo, which makes the point of view of europe heard and does not suffer the initiatives of the american administration.
mr president, europe is strongly committed to peace but is deeply frustrated by the latest terrorist acts. for it, the mediterranean is a crucial area, a historic challenge, and until it is transformed into an area of peace, development, democracy and stability, europe will unfortunately not be able to realise its full potential. today the conflict between israel and palestine is closing every window of hope, and its intensification is certainly not facilitating the fight against international terrorism, but is actually feeding it ideologically. but the future of peace in this area requires greater responsibility and we must commit all our forces, our political dignity, our financial and human resources to resuming the negotiating process. the athens forum was therefore a very important step, but it is not a question of being partisan of either side; the way forward, even now that hopes for peace seem almost dead, remains to work towards a clear goal: two states, side by side, israel and palestine, living in peace and security without prejudice and with respect for human rights. this is why europe must work in constant and open dialogue with the entire arab world.
council. mr president, i will be very brief. i should like to thank the members who have participated. this has been a useful discussion, and it has also made clear that there exists a fundamental consensus amongst us on the need for the european union to exert maximum effort on the issues that have been raised. we all agree that the european union needs to raise the level of its engagement within the region. i agree particularly with members as to the importance of intensive consultation with the countries involved in the region. the drive and the initiative to address these challenges and to implement the necessary reforms must come from the region itself. it is easy to be in despair when you see, as we have seen, horror heaped on horror, misery on top of misery. however, the reality is that the union must be involved in pushing forward where it can to try and create at least a space in which peace can be created. the union must work in partnership with the region to this end, to help to meet all of these challenges. a lasting and comprehensive settlement is set out in the roadmap, and the roadmap is the only way forward. it was mentioned that the suspension of the association agreement is a way forward in the region. i think that discussion and pressure would produce some progress. in the interim report on the strategic partnership endorsed by the council last week, the european union has established a set of guiding principles for its engagement in the region. the presidency is committed to developing the strategic partnership on the basis of partnership and dialogue with all the countries in the region. once again, i should like to thank members for their thoughtful and incisive contributions.

mr president, the new tragedies in kosovo demonstrate, as we have repeatedly said and reminded this parliament and the council, that peace and democracy are not something that has been achieved definitively even on the european continent, but are objectives that need to be reaffirmed every day. it is not only peace and democracy that are in question, but also the dignity of the person and respect for the common cultural heritage. the destruction of monasteries in kosovo, which was carried out with the sole aim of destroying the symbols of a culture that has the same roots as the european union, shows that international terrorism and the guerrilla warfare it unleashes in some countries are based on a specific political plan, against which our inaction is not only a sign of dangerous weakness but also a harbinger of new tragedies. the union must step up its presence in kosovo, calling on the albanian, serbian and montenegrin authorities to preserve the rights of minorities and to protect the sites of serbian orthodox culture. it is pointless to have mobilized, and rightly so, against the destruction of the buddhas in afghanistan by the taliban if so many orthodox churches and monuments - that is, belonging to a culture which is the expression of the values we strive to preserve - are being destroyed in the guilty silence of intellectuals and politicians. the precious frescoes which have adorned the temples and monasteries of the balkan orthodox church for centuries are the heritage of all humanity and must be safeguarded and preserved from any risk of destructive madness, as we have stressed in our resolution. the italian foreign minister has done well in making more men available to nato to reinforce the contingent responsible for maintaining order and guaranteeing security. the un should, for its part, provide for a programme of reconstruction of destroyed property and define the broad lines of kosovo's final status; but we also want a stronger european presence, and for this reason we recall that a european army, as called for by the convention, is an objective that can no longer be postponed.
mr president, commissioner, i think that the speeches by my colleagues, especially those of my group but also others, have put the spotlight on the fundamental problem we have in this report. the extension of the right to information is not in question, which we all support, of course. the convention is not in question, because that is not what it is about, as has been said, since it was signed. the commission has tried to make the convention viable, and this is well documented by the speeches of some colleagues and the submission of amendments: i am not saying that the commission should not be involved in the negotiations, but that it should be involved in the negotiations with the council and the commission. i am convinced that this reason stems from a healthy concern, from the good intentions of the commissioner and his colleagues, but we must be very vigilant in this respect, because we risk embarking on an action to delegitimize our democratic institutions, which are the elected representatives, the governors, the assemblies at municipal and local level, in short, that system of democratic guarantees which are the only sovereign ones able to represent the interests of the citizens in their entirety. now, the fact that we want to pay special attention to the environment is right and proper and it is absolutely desirable to involve all citizens. but be careful not to go beyond the limit of the so-called special legislation: when i hear mr schörling speak of no time or geographical limits, the risk of creating counter-powers becomes serious, and if that risk were to materialise in tomorrow's final text, i think we would do well, as some colleagues have said, to vote against it.
mr president, it has been said that this european council was one of renewed cohesion in the wake of the terrible tragedy in madrid, which makes it so clear that we are all potential victims of blind again expressing our scepticism about an approach which this european council and, alas, the european parliament in its resolution, which we cannot support on this part, have endorsed, which makes competitiveness an absolute dogma. but who do we have to be competitive against? ask the voters who have just spoken in france and spain, ask them what they think about the systems and the working sustainable growth that we continue to leave aside, if we decided to invest in sectors of the future, such as research, clean energy, eco-tourism and public transport. these are our demands, the path we ask you to take to revive growth. more than bridges and highways!
as regards the coordinator for counter-terrorism, mr president, i believe that the shortcut to cooperation between national bureaucracies in the absence of democratic and judicial control is an illusion, just as for europol and eurojust it is not just a matter of individual rights and freedoms, although serious and serious, but also a matter of effectiveness: in fact, you failed to give real powers to this coordinator of the fight against terrorism. so this is not consistent with those who want a more effective struggle, a more rapid reaction, who want not less democracy - which is the illusion of all bureaucracies - but more democracy and more law. if the creation of mr antiterrorism is to be the paradigm of the new constitution we want, a modest increase in power at the cost of giving up democracy and the rule of law is not the way we want to go. one last word on iraq: the european union invokes the un, but it invokes it to cover its own incapacity. the terrorists have a political agenda, a political objective, the withdrawal of europe or what is left of europe from iraq. we would have liked the european union to have had the strength to choose, to propose, to decide the opposite of what the terrorists want.

mr president, on this subject the european commission has chosen an illegitimate procedure, which sequesters the powers of this parliament by making a real transfer of european sovereignty to the united states of america. i therefore ask the commission at this point whether, instead of pretending to have us vote, it does not think it would be better to leave the matter to the only democratic institution with decisionmaking power involved in this matter, namely the congress of the united states of america.
in the case of the european parliament, the council has not yet adopted a decision on the proposal for a council framework decision on the european arrest warrant for the purpose of obtaining objects, documents and data for use in criminal proceedings. as with the european arrest warrant, we simply do not believe that the integration of criminal law and the application of the principle of mutual recognition of criminal measures and decisions between member states can be achieved without at the same time strengthening security and the right of defence in europe. the commission had promised - as the european parliament has repeatedly requested - to adopt a framework decision on the protection of the rights of suspects and accused persons, but this proposal is currently on hold. in a situation such as the one described, we would have preferred the solution of rejecting the proposal in question or making its entry into force conditional on the adoption of the framework decision on the protection of rights, so as to ensure full respect for the guarantees of criminal procedure which are an essential part of the effective protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms in europe.
in the light of the recent events in the united states, the commission has decided to withdraw its proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks arising from exposure to carcinogens. the decision states that the commission's decision to assess the us protection of privacy as adequate in relation to the processing of passenger data collected in european territory is incorrect, that the commission has exceeded its implementing powers and that it calls on the commission to withdraw its decision. the resolution also states that parliament has the possibility of referring the matter to the court of justice to determine whether the draft international agreement with the united states is legitimate. in our opinion, today the european parliament has courageously - and despite the strong pressure from the commission, national governments and the united states - played its full role as the representative of the european citizens and as the defender of their freedoms and rights, as well as of democracy and the rule of law.

i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. first of all, i would like to express my satisfaction that the process of croatia's application for membership of the european union has now moved forward with a concrete perspective of opening negotiations. i can only agree with what mrs pack said and confirm how much croatia is trying to get closer to the european union. i have also been following developments in croatia since 1995, first as head of delegation and then as chairman of the eu/slovenia joint parliamentary committee, and i have found that the steadily increasing efforts have been moving in the right direction. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject. the new government of ivo sanader has really set milestones in this area, especially as regards minority policy and the coexistence of ethnic groups. let me say this as a representative of a minority. this form of action by the new government and the prime minister in particular commands very, very much respect. it could also serve as an example for other candidate countries and be shown to be exemplary in terms of new access and openness between majority and minority peoples of whatever kind. the international arbitration process has also helped to ensure that positive considerations prevail in the case of border disputes or border disputes in the gulf of piran. i believe that this is the right way to go, since both countries have legitimate interests, and so this issue need not be a stumbling block anymore. i would like to thank mr baltas for his excellent report and hope that the commission will do its part to open the negotiations.
mr president, i find the baltas report very balanced and logical in its main lines. the climate has become much calmer - which is very important in those parts - in the last period. president mesic in belgrade apologizes to those who had suffered pain or harm at the hands of croatian citizens. the serbian president, in turn, apologizes for the evil acts committed by serbian citizens against croats. it is easier to achieve reconciliation through these gestures, even though only a few years have passed since a very bitter and very hard war. prime minister sanader visits the serbian minority during the christmas holidays and then talks to the italian minority in italian: all this would not have been possible years ago. the same harshness is no longer present in relations with slovenia. and this is the context that helps resolve disputes about arrest warrants and the return of refugees. if this is the context, then one can also reason about the unilateral decision of croatia to declare a part of its coast as an ecological and biological zone. in his report, mr baltas states that this unilateral decision by croatia has hindered the practice of dialogue and consultation, which is one of the cornerstones of dealing with problems of regional impact. there are, of course, difficulties in zagreb's initiating its economic and political reforms. let us remember that between 2000 and 2002 inflation fell from 7.4 per cent to 2.3 per cent, and we know what that means. however, on a scale published by the frankfurter allgemeine zeitung, which measures the performance of the candidate countries, this year's and future ones, the first candidate countries to join the european union in may total a score of 80%, while croatia is at 50%. so there is still a lot of work to be done. i think there is a danger - if it was mentioned earlier - of seeing people disappointed: the european union is perhaps disappointed that croatia is not joining the union immediately, but mr baltas points out that it will take a lot of perseverance and a lot of time for croatia to have the means to ensure the consolidation of the rule of law and democracy.

vice-president of the commission. (es) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, mr coelho, for his report. this proposal for a regulation follows the path set in 1999 by the directive on vehicle registration documents, with a view to combating the illicit traffic in stolen vehicles, and should improve the functioning of the internal market by providing vehicle registration services with better access to the largest european database of stolen vehicles, namely the schengen information system. we all know that there are very important financial interests at stake, because every year millions of cars are registered in the member states of the union and billions of these billions are stolen. the proposal also aims to improve the safety of citizens and thus the area of freedom, security and justice by introducing more effective mechanisms to combat vehicle theft. it provides in particular for the possibility that, before registering a vehicle, the national services responsible may, under certain conditions, consult the sis database of stolen vehicles and registration documents and check whether the vehicle in question has already been registered in another of the states signatory to the schengen agreement. this information will make it more difficult for thieves to sell stolen vehicles in another 'schengen state' and at the same time increase the chances of victims being able to recover the lost vehicle. the authorities currently lack the resources to verify the provenance of the over one million vehicles registered each year. the proposal under discussion will allow systematic access to the database, and thus to search for vehicles and registration documents, and to have access to it will have to comply with a number of common rules on transparency and a number of principles on data protection. the rules on the protection of personal data shall be laid down in the sis ii legal instruments. in the interests of data protection, it has been proposed to give different access to public services and to private services responsible for vehicle registration. the officials of the responsible public services should have the right to query the schengen database directly, while, despite performing a function specific to the public administration, when private services carry out this activity, they will have restricted access to it, through the police, like the rest of the existing vehicle registration services. i would like to thank the rapporteur and parliament once again for the work they have done.
mr president, i thank the commission representative and the rapporteur for being very clear in their views. it is inevitable that when databases are created containing vast amounts of information, there will be a temptation to use them for a wide variety of purposes. this can be useful and legitimate under certain conditions, in particular in compliance with the fundamental right of citizens to the protection of personal data, enshrined in article 8 of the charter of rights of the union. the need has now arisen for vehicle registration services to be able to obtain all the necessary information collected in the schengen information system in order to verify that the vehicles are not stolen and that the applicant's documents are authentic. the report also calls for the commission to take a more active role in the fight against fraud and for the commission to be able to take action to combat fraud. even with these limitations, the proposal for a regulation remains useful. what i do not agree with is that the information is not provided by the police and customs authorities responsible for storing and processing schengen data, but is directly acquired by the public registration offices with direct access to the sis databases. such access by the many vehicle registration offices in the 25 member states would seriously jeopardise the protection of personal data entered into the schengen system. the nature of the services responsible for the safety of citizens, the rules and controls to which they are subject, the purposes for which they may use the data they come to know are very different from those of the administrative offices, public or not, which deal with the registration of vehicles. moreover, there is no apparent need to allow direct access to schengen data, since it would not be permitted to the same registration offices which, according to the rules of the different countries, are private rather than public. this would lead to a disparity of rules which would increase confusion rather than improve the effectiveness of the measure. it is therefore better to regulate the use of schengen data by registration services, allowing everyone equal indirect access, which avoids risks of abuse and respects the principle of proportionality.

commission. mr president, i would like to express the commission's disappointment at the position proposed in mr lisi's report and voted. in fact our proposal implements the political guidelines in the mediterranean action plan which was welcomed by parliament and adopted by council. several of the critical comments made are simply not correct. i would like to make a few points here. firstly, the commission proposal does give flexibility to member state managers when deciding on different options to manage fish stocks in the mediterranean, so we do take into consideration the characteristics of mediterranean fisheries. the proof is that national administrations can establish national management plans and these plans may allow derogations from certain common provisions precisely because of the different situations in different parts of the mediterranean. secondly, we are well aware that the fisheries sector is opposed to our proposal. we have received similar reactions to the recovery plan for cod in the northern waters of the community. the difference here, however, was that both the sector and the european parliament had been open to dialogue with the commission and we adopted an acceptable plan in the end. and it is precisely this spirit we also need for the southern waters. thirdly, the alleged poor consultation of the sector does not correspond to the truth. the sector has been consulted on several occasions before and after we presented our proposal. fourthly, the commission agrees with parliament that we need to work with other mediterranean countries, and that is why we took the initiative for the venice conference in november 2003 when the mediterranean countries, ranging from morocco to lebanon and slovenia, agreed with the european union that we must agree on common measures in the general fisheries commission for the mediterranean to preserve our fish stocks. finally, i would like to make one thing very clear. the disastrous social and economic costs mr lisi mentioned in his report will be the result if we do not take prompt and effective action as proposed by us, because the state of many mediterranean fish stocks is already quite poor. we cannot, therefore, accept your request to withdraw our proposal, because there are neither scientific nor political justifications for parliament's request. the rapporteur. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the proposal for a directive on unfair commercial practices largely follows the green paper on consumer protection, to which the commissioner referred in his report. the reasons underlying this directive, which aims at a high level of consumer protection, are essentially economic in nature and therefore relate to the completion and proper functioning of the internal market. these broad economic objectives require a consumer protection policy to be achieved which can create confidence among european consumers and thus give impetus to transnational and european commercial transactions. the advantage of economic growth in the internal market must translate into both higher profits for firms and a high level of protection and economic advantage for consumers, and through a common consumer policy and the harmonization of the internal market price convergence can also be achieved. on the contrary, unfair commercial practices induce irrational economic behaviour on the part of consumers, distort competition and hamper the proper functioning of the market. the commission's approach, based on harmonisation, the principle of mutual recognition of the country of origin, the provision of a general clause prohibiting unfair commercial practices, the identification of specific criteria and cases and the limitation of the scope of action to a framework directive based on relations between undertakings and consumers, is therefore acceptable. the creation of a european regulatory framework in terms of unfair commercial practices, based on an appropriate level of harmonisation and aimed at clear and effective consumer protection within the union, will result in developing consumer confidence, increasing transnational trade and thus allowing businesses to compete in conditions of legal clarity. while agreeing with the basic approach and the main objectives of the directive, parliament proposes a number of amendments which, in our view, make a number of definitions and options clearer and more unambiguous. the commission is proposing to add to the legal base proposed by the commission on the internal market that relating to article 153 on consumer protection, in order to reconcile the objective of harmonisation and mutual recognition of the internal market with the possibility for member states to maintain legislation which goes further in consumer protection. i believe that this is a response to the fears of those who believe that maximum harmonization could lead to a downgrading of consumer protection or a step backwards from the legislation already in force in many member states. we also believe that it is important in relation to the definition of consumers to refer to a specific group of consumers, in particular vulnerable consumers, who need, for different factors such as age, physical or mental condition, level of literacy or disabilities, higher protection. i think it is important - and i will put it back to the house - to have an amendment on the definition of professional diligence. we share the commission's approach to the regulation of deceptive and aggressive practices in substance, but we stress that consumer information on unfair commercial practices and codes of conduct is a key element. we also think that the distinction between national and community codes of conduct is a bit fictitious, and therefore we point out the need to provide for appropriate and effective mechanisms to monitor and enforce compliance with them. another important point, which refers to codes of conduct as a method of settling and resolving disputes between businesses and consumers, is arbitration, which cannot and should not replace or prevent recourse to administrative bodies, but can be an important factor. the adoption of this directive and of tomorrow's report is therefore, as my colleagues also recalled and as the commissioner has stressed, absolutely important both for the development of the internal market and for consumer protection. i would like to thank all those who have contributed to improving this document and to finding a solution. i would like to thank in particular mr paciotti, who has taken over for me during a period of absence for health reasons and who has conducted the work on my behalf very well.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, consumer protection is an important objective and we must prevent dishonest operators from taking advantage of the internal market to deceive citizens by offering them defective products. moreover, dishonest operators exploit the new technologies offered by the information society to their advantage, thus undermining consumer confidence in electronic commerce. these problems are of concern not only to consumers but also to businesses and the economy as a whole. in order to guarantee the consumer, therefore, it is necessary, in addition to creating a body of clear and effective rules, to improve cooperation between national authorities responsible for consumer protection, by encouraging the exchange of information and providing for the possibility for the authorities addressed by the consumer to request the intervention of authorities in another country. the proposal for a regulation which parliament is debating and voting on this week is aimed at this second important objective. the national systems in place today are not suited to preventing and combating cross-border abuse, and the existing informal networks are faced with legal obstacles linked in particular to difficulties in the flow of information from one authority to another and even to the lack of public authorities in charge of consumer protection in some member states. the regulation provides, inter alia, for the obligation for the member states to designate a public authority responsible for consumer protection, which will form part of a european network. the authority of a member state may ask the other members of the network for assistance in investigating any wrongdoing, in receiving information and thus putting an end to abuses by dishonest operators. the legal affairs committee has taken steps to make the proposed regulation a little more protective, to avoid abuses by national authorities, to protect confidential information held by companies that is not strictly necessary for the investigation, to ensure that requests for cooperation are reasonably justified and do not unnecessarily hinder economic operators. the proposal for a regulation also contains procedures which are sometimes a bit cumbersome and onerous for the consumer and for the public administration, and which risk making the costs far greater than the damage suffered by the consumer. we therefore oppose certain provisions proposed by the council and the commission which would make the system very complicated and if we are to adopt the regulation in its pre-enlargement reading, we will ensure that it is revised, on the basis of experience, five years after its entry into force.
the rapporteur. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the european commission has presented the communication to the council and the european parliament on the modernisation of company law and the strengthening of corporate governance in the union. this communication is a response to the report of 4 november 2002 by the group of high-level experts on company law, which commissioner bolkestein had appointed with the aim of indicating a modern regulatory framework for company law in europe. the communication sets out the major policy objectives which will guide any future initiative. it includes an action plan setting out a timetable of a series of regulatory and non-regulatory instruments to be implemented in the short, medium and long term, together with a grid indicating the level of priority of the measures. in presenting this proposal, the commission took account of the need to respect the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality, and gradually adopted its regulatory approach, while keeping in mind the evolution of regulation at international level. the commission has two main objectives in its efforts to reform company law in europe: the strengthening of shareholder rights and the protection of third parties; the promotion of efficiency and competitiveness of enterprises. the main reasons for this initiative can be found in the determination of a more homogeneous framework of reference in the field of company law to facilitate the exercise of cross-border settlement and restructuring; the commission is therefore proposing to the environment; the environment; the environment; the environment; the environment; the environment; the environment to achieve the first of the objectives set out in the action plan, it is essential to ensure an effective and up-to-date system of protection of shareholders and their rights. the position of certain third parties, particularly qualified in view of the interests they represent, must also be taken into account: creditors and workers. from this point of view, the commission's approach to the governance problem appears reductive and partial. the issue of corporate governance is presented as a problem limited to the relationship between shareholders and management, as if an enterprise were an entity that could only be referred to the interests of shareholders. this, i believe, is how a virtual company is described, without giving adequate prominence to the workers and without more general responsibilities towards other categories of stakeholders in the life of the company itself, such as, for example, creditors and other stakeholders. with regard to the second objective, the competitiveness of enterprises can be enhanced by a number of factors, one of which is the need for an appropriate regulatory framework in the field of company law, which is well balanced between national and community measures. i believe that the challenge to be faced today is to bring out the distinctive and peculiar features of european capitalism in the age of globalisation. in other words, we must see whether we can speak of a genuine european model which differs significantly from the north american model, not because it corresponds to and is based on a different set of challenges and problems than those facing everyone in the context of economic globalization, but because it pays attention to the social implications and implications, so as to make it more temperate and more humane: a model which thus seeks to conceive the essential core of the corporate model, represented by the social interest, not only as the common interest of the members and shareholders but as the interest of the undertaking itself, an undertaking which, as an autonomous economic actor, also takes care of the particular interest of all those outside and inside the undertaking itself, such as employees, customers, suppliers, creditors, the public administration, as a provider of welfare or taxation, and the whole of civil society, which is structurally linked with the general and common interest in the prosperity and continuity of the undertaking. a final general point: the right balance between self-regulation and heteregulation needs to be struck when designing the framework for european governance. there is a widespread tendency today to fill in the regulatory gaps with self-regulatory codes. all this is good, but it is not enough. a system of legally binding rules, with effective sanctions, enforced by public bodies to which the tasks of direction and supervision are entrusted, should be maintained. the perspective must be precisely to globalise the rules of governance and to network the public supervisory authorities. we have therefore focused our attention on a few key points, which i will just mention: the commission has also adopted a communication on the implementation of the european union strategy for growth and jobs, which sets out the main priorities for the period 2007-2013.
madam president, commissioner, rapporteurs, in june last year the european union adopted, with difficulty and much later than the requirements of european consumers and businesses, directive 2003/55/ec, which moves decisively towards completing the liberalisation of the gas market. in order to overcome the remaining obstacles to the completion of the internal market, the member states had set up an informal coordination between the community and national institutions on the one hand and the gas industry and regulatory authorities on the other, in an attempt to define guidelines on third party access to transmission networks. however, this informal coordination has proved inadequate to address the serious delays accumulated to date; in fact, the interests in companies from several countries and the position rents that many gas distribution companies continue to earn have seriously jeopardized the possibility of completing a single market in gas distribution. the commission's initiative to propose the regulation we are discussing today, which makes the operational rules clearer and more binding and responds to the need to provide certainty and speed to consumers and businesses, should therefore be welcomed. the text which has been drawn up by the committee on industry and research improves this proposal even further and i therefore believe that it should be supported as it stands. it is in the interest of certain countries - i am thinking in particular of italy - which are characterized by high natural gas consumption, insufficient national production and a strong and growing dependence on imports, which require many countries to cross, to have a situation of certain rules which imply, from an operational point of view, the effective possibility of having a market and, therefore, a system of liberalized networks making gas available to businesses and consumers to the extent and in the right quantity, with freedom of choice.

(es) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, you have before you today a very important dossier in the framework of european transport policy, namely the proposal to amend the 'eurovignette' directive. as you know, the current community framework is not sufficient in view of the numerous initiatives which have recently been taken at national level in this regard. this framework needs to be strengthened, in particular to ensure a level playing field between carriers and to give greater impetus to the balance between the different modes of transport. i would first of all like to congratulate mr cocilovo on his excellent work. it has achieved something which - i must say - seemed to me impossible: to group, to conflate, to integrate a series of amendments, presenting in the end a result which respects the principles and philosophy of the commission's proposal. i would like to put a few technical points aside and make it clear that the commission supports parliament's report. first of all, i would point out that the draft report endorses a fundamental principle of the proposal, namely the allocation of the revenue from charging to transport. this principle has been and continues to be the subject of intense debate in the council. it is therefore vitally important that parliament has supported it. how can the collection of a toll on a motorway be justified if it is not known where the funds it generates will go? in a single market such as we have now, how can this situation be justified which could lead us back to almost medieval times? the implementation of major projects in the transeuropean transport network is also hampered by a lack of funds. road pricing is thus a financial incentive mechanism which should help to improve connections in the enlarged union. as regards the calculation of tolls, the draft report encourages the commission to develop a method for calculating external costs. the discussions in the council show that it has not been possible to reach a compromise based on objective data and objectively determined costs. we must continue to work on this and find a reliable methodology that can be clearly accepted horizontally within the council. it is also necessary to obtain parliament's support for the increase of tolls in particularly sensitive areas, such as mountainous areas and those affected by a high level of pollution. this latter concept is totally innovative and will contribute to the realisation of costly but necessary infrastructures for the elimination of bottlenecks in our network. i would like to end by stressing that the commission has made every effort to present the european parliament with a text that is acceptable to all; transit countries must be able to apply a charging system that enables them to promote their own network in the context of sustainable transport development, and at the same time the peripheral countries of the union and their carriers will be the first to benefit from an effective charging policy in europe that provides them with a viable network. on the other hand, we must understand the concerns of those countries which see a possible increase in the cost of transport and therefore a comparative disadvantage. in order not to affect the viability of transport undertakings, tolls must be set at reasonable levels corresponding to the consideration for the use of an infrastructure, and therefore to the objectively determined costs generated by the use of that infrastructure. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, as you know, i would have liked to have seen this initiative adopted at first reading and an informal trilogue, or an informal agreement between parliament, the council and the commission, to have allowed this initiative to be taken forward. it was not possible. however, i hope that, thanks to the outcome of the cocilovo report, the amendments which are to be introduced and the fundamental support of this parliament for the commission's proposal, we will soon be able to reach a common position of the council which is close enough to parliament's proposal to allow the adoption of this very important directive for the balance of transport in the european union to be completed quickly, either at second reading or in a rapid conciliation.
the rapporteur. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, as the commissioner has said, i would like to stress not only the importance of this proposal for a directive in the development of a european strategy for the transport system, but also to point out that this is not the first time that the commission, the european parliament and the council have had the opportunity to deal with the issues of the transport system and networks and to make specific contributions in this regard, also with reference to the external costs generated by their use and the consequent problems linked, in particular, to the pricing of transport infrastructure. as regards the reference to parliament's contribution, on all these occasions, in the conclusions relating to the individual reports and individual interventions, the need was highlighted first of all to harmonize transport tariffs in all the member states in order to consolidate the european single market; the possibility of transforming - in some cases in an objective and realistic way, if not radically, and at the same time with considerable effort - the structure of fares in order to allow the internationalisation of the external costs of transport, which are often overlooked in decisions on modal choices and thus in infrastructure construction strategies; the need, however, to develop a charging system commensurate with the use of transport infrastructure; the need to make the mechanisms for calculating these costs uniform for each mode of transport and, of course, in the relationship between member states; finally, the need to give governments transport policy instruments based on the cost of infrastructure to encourage the use of modes of transport with a lower environmental impact, but above all to allow new investment in the infrastructure sector and, in the long term, therefore, the completion of the design linked to the full implementation of the major infrastructure projects for the transeuropean transport networks, which also suffer from the problems of financial coverage referred to a little earlier and which, moreover, we all know. at this stage, however, the governments of the member states are confronted with two distinct phenomena: on the one hand, there is an increasing urgency to invest large resources to meet the demand for transport infrastructure. on the other hand, the cost problems - also of a social nature - that the growth of mobility entails, from increased pollution to environmental problems, the increase in noise, especially congestion levels and the accident rate, must be addressed. i do not recall the figures for this prospect, even in the context of enlargement and the foreseeable increase in demand, which will still have to be met. the problem is not just the external costs. we know that the construction of infrastructure and the operation of the transport system have often been financed mainly through general taxation. today, however, the new approach to the public sector, the constraints on member states' budgets, privatization policies and services mean that private capital must also be used to build infrastructure, or else a supplementary design will have to be abandoned. i would just like to point out that if all this is to be done outside the perspective - which is unacceptable to anyone - of a prejudicial penalisation of road transport, alternatives must be guaranteed, because the sole lever of tolling, or of intervention on costs, in the absence of real and competitive alternatives to redirect part of the volume of traffic, would not be an answer to the problem but would aggravate, from the point of view of costs, with general economic consequences, the compulsory use of these infrastructures. it is precisely in this sense that we have tried to give guidance and to find solutions to the problem, in the most sensitive and hot spots, through the instrument of cost financing and to divert an additional set of resources to accelerate the implementation of these projects. i conclude by regretting that a certain delay in the discussion of positions within the council is preventing a complete result today, to the attainment of which i believe that the strengthening of parliament's position can make a great contribution to the future development of the negotiations, and in this connection i must thank all the groups and all the colleagues for the valuable collaboration given to this work at this stage, at least with a view to the objective of concluding the first reading of the report positively.

the commission (es) the honourable member will recall that the directives establishing the internal market in electricity contain general principles and lay down minimum legal provisions. however, member states are free to apply these directives in the manner they deem most appropriate to meet national conditions and needs and to lay down stricter minimum standards, provided that they comply with community rules and, in particular, the principles of the treaty. in other words, we leave room for subsidiarity so that we can adapt and apply the directives in the most appropriate way, taking into account the reality and diversity of the various member states. the commission considers it essential to ensure a common level of effective opening of the electricity markets of the various member states on equal terms. in this context, following the adoption of directive 96/92/ec, i.e. the first electricity directive, the commission published an annual benchmarking report examining the implementation of the internal market in gas and electricity in all the member states of the union. these reports showed a significant difference between the levels of effective market opening. this led the commission to propose a second package of legislative measures to complete the internal market in electricity, to avoid the tensions and distortions that were occurring, and these measures include directive 2003/54/ec and regulation 1228/2003, which were adopted by parliament and the council last june and will enter into force as of july 2004. the new legislation provides for much higher common minimum standards for market opening both in quantitative terms, i.e. minimum level of market opening, and in qualitative terms: legal separation, regulated third party access, etc. as regards quantitative measures, two stages are envisaged: a first stage, july 2004, when the market for non-household consumers must be opened to competition, and 100% of the market in july 2007. in any case, this will reduce the asymmetries which exist today between the various markets in the european union and make it possible to achieve what has been the aim since the first directive: the integration of the european electricity and gas market into a single market, ending the frontiers which still exist today and avoiding something which would not be desirable: 15 markets or 25 markets as from 1 may for electricity and gas. this is not the goal. the aim is to create a single, integrated european market for electricity and gas.
first of all, commissioner, i would like to thank you for the information you have given me. however, could the commission say how the application of these minimum standards is monitored and to what extent countermeasures are taken if these minimum standards are not respected?

mr president, i fully agree with the rapporteur. contrary to what the commission claims, the draft agreement which has been submitted to us is in radical contradiction with the directive on the protection of personal data and therefore with article 286 of the ec treaty. while article 7 of the directive confers on the member states the power to regulate the processing of personal data for security reasons, in the draft agreement the community takes that power away from the member states and confers it on a foreign authority. the draft law provides that european airlines will be obliged to process european passenger data as required by the us department of homeland security's customs bureau, under us law. this amendment to the directive, which the commission insists on denying, would have entailed, inter alia, that parliament - the co-legislator of the directive - would have to give its assent under article 300 of the treaty, as commissioner bolkestein had already announced to the committee on civil liberties on 9 september, which is not the case. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented. in addition, the project violates one of the fundamental rights of european citizens, namely the right to privacy of personal data, as set out in the european convention on human rights and the charter of fundamental rights of the european union. this right provides, inter alia, that data subjects may access and obtain rectification of the data collected, which is not guaranteed in the draft agreement, and this is relevant from a practical point of view, because data collected for commercial purposes, and not checked or filtered by a responsible authority, may be inaccurate or even deliberately unrealistic. these discrepancies - which would not occur in the case of data knowingly transmitted for security purposes - can cause serious inconvenience and real harm to our citizens. it is therefore necessary to change the text of the agreement and, in any case, to seek the opinion of the court of justice. commissioner patten, i believe that the european union is in a weak position because we have given up applying our own rules. the draft agreement is contrary not only to the directive but also to the fundamental principles of the european union. even if it were a member state, it would be illegal to systematically use data collected for commercial and surveillance purposes. that's the problem! it is clear that we must fight terrorism and it is clear that we must do so with rules which respect the fundamental principles of our european union. what is false is that the suspension of compliance with the law, which has now been in force for more than 13 months, is somehow imposed on our airlines. it is not, because this measure applies to all airlines, even american airlines when booking in europe. so you decided to take a weak and losing position. you can't change the rules. you can't reform when you don't respect the form!

the committee on constitutional affairs has adopted a report on the proposal for a directive on the protection of the european parliament and of the council against the proliferation of weapons of mass destruction. mr president, i am speaking in place of mr voggenhuber, draftsman of the opinion of the committee on constitutional affairs. the committee on constitutional affairs adopted its opinion unanimously, which is divided into three main points: the first point is the conflict of interest, where it is hoped - i repeat, it is hoped - unanimously that there will be legislation to prohibit political figures or candidates from having significant economic interests in the media sector; the second point states that it is necessary to define a procedure whereby parliament can, in an autonomous way, initiate autonomously, i repeat procedures which make it possible to conduct an inquiry into the situation of fundamental rights in a member state, in accordance with article 7 (1) of our treaty; the third point is that the committee on constitutional affairs considers that any action by a member state which restricts freedom of expression and freedom of information within the member state is a violation of this article. i would like to stress that there is no intention of persecution but simply the unanimous will, at least of the committee on constitutional affairs, to define a procedure for how the european parliament, the commission and the council should act in such sensitive cases.

mr president, i would like to thank the rapporteur for her valuable work in a competent and impartial manner. i would like to thank you in particular for your great patience in the face of the unjustified polemics and obstructionism of the last few days, which were certainly not motivated by the interest in protecting a fundamental right of european citizens. the report, drawn up with the authorisation of the conference of presidents and in accordance with the instructions given by it, deals with the subject of freedom and political and cultural pluralism of the media, which are essential elements for a fully-fledged democracy and which, in turn, constitute a founding value of the european union, in accordance with article 6 of the treaty. the report, which is fully acceptable, analyses the criteria to be respected, the instruments that can be adopted to ensure freedom of information and pluralism, and suggests ways in which the community and the member states can intervene, while respecting the principle of subsidiarity. this analytical and propositional work is of great importance for the future of an enlarged community of 25 member states. the report also examines the risks of infringement of freedom of expression and information and, as required in the mandate given to the commission, particularly in italy. risks are everywhere, always, but it is difficult to deny the italian anomaly due to objective and undeniable factors: the highest concentration rate in europe in the audiovisual market, characterised by the duopoly of a public body, rai, and a private group, mediaset, which collects 96.8 per cent of advertising resources, by the fact that the mediaset group is controlled by the current president of the council of ministers, by the fact that in italy the organisation chart and the programming of the public television service are influenced by the government and its majority, by the fact that the conflict of interests of the president of the council is still unregulated. moreover, as is shown by numerous judgments of the italian constitutional court, the italian broadcasting system has been operating for years in an unlawful situation, which the legislator has not yet remedied; in fact, the laws passed so far, which ratified the de facto occupation of frequencies, i.e. the operation of facilities without authorization and concessions by the largest private group, outside any logic of increasing pluralism, have been gradually declared unconstitutional, and the latest law reforming the audiovisual sector was sent back to the chambers by the president of the republic because it did not comply with the principles affirmed by the constitutional court. in the face of these undeniable facts, no one can fail to see the seriousness of the risk of concrete and repeated violation of the fundamental right to pluralism of information that european citizens in italy are running.
mr president, it is surprising and amusing that many of the figures cited, which are called into question and whose proof is sought, are mentioned on the mediaset website. but is it possible that mediaset is lying? mr president, we have finally reached the debate in the house on mrs boogerd-quaak's report: the report is an excellent one, and it is the result of the concern shared by the vast majority of parliament for freedom of information and expression in the european union. of course, we would have preferred that the commission had moved forward with a specific proposal for a directive during these five years. the situation is becoming more and more worrying in all the countries of the union, as a result of massive concentrations of the media and increasingly overt attempts by governments to direct, dominate and use the media in order to maintain and strengthen their own power. the italian example is the one which worries us most, both because of the damage it has done to freedom of information and because of the model it offers to other european countries. europe cannot continue to remain silent in the face of the scandal of a president of the council who owns the three private television networks, owns the largest television advertising collection company, controls the three public networks and puts all his political sympathizers in charge of them, even publicly announcing the dismissal of journalists such as enzo biagi and michele santoro, whom he dislikes. in this increasingly neoliberal and anti-democratic europe, in which media power plays a functional role in the unification of thought, in wars, in the dismantling of the welfare state, it is necessary to give a signal of change or, at least, of rethinking. the boogerd-quaak report on the media is moving in this direction and will therefore have the support of our group, the european confederal left/nordic green left.
mr president, this report has three objectives: the first is to reiterate once again that the majority of this parliament believes that dealing with freedom of expression in the media is entirely within the competence of the european union and that action is therefore necessary because this issue could become a real problem - a risk, in fact - for democracy. this is not a new theme. this time it has been dealt with in a more specific and elaborate way, more substantiated by examples, but it is not very different from what parliament had voted unanimously a while ago when it asked the commission to take action. this activation has never been done before, and i would like to know the reasons for it, if you know them, commissioner patten. the second objective is to give some concrete examples of these risks, which certainly exist in various countries but which are demonstrated in italy in a most blatant way, which simply lies in the concentration of political, media and economic power in one person, something which - from a political, cultural and legislative point of view - would be practically impossible in any other western democracy. the third point i would like to make about this report is the recommendations that are made. these are recommendations to the countries, and in particular to italy, which are not very different from the conclusions of the report referred to by mr santini, namely the resolution of the conflict of interests and the resolution of the concentration of media power in italy between two parties, and, i repeat this again because it seems particularly important to me, the request to the commission to take action to legislate on this issue. i repeat my question to commissioner patten: i would like to know what the commission has done with this request and what it will do, since this text will certainly be adopted.
