mr president, you know that over the years we have developed a continuous and even radical criticism of the way in which our europe has been built. i say "our" because we are europeanists, like you. i would therefore like to ask you a question which we should all consider together, rather than repeat our criticisms, even if they are harsh, of the work of your commission, criticisms which you are already aware of. how does the construction of europe compare with liberal globalisation? can we be fooled into believing that a gradual process subordinate to this globalisation is good, without seeing that it is devouring the rights acquired in the social states of the twentieth century and is building new global dynamics of injustice and democratic expropriation? we have even the return of war, the spiral of terrorism, or the pitting of workers against each other, as is being attempted these days in the automobile industry. where is europe in this picture? and is not its own constitution a step backwards from a constitution like the italian one, founded on work and the rejection of war, and almost an accompaniment to the dictates of liberal globalization? that is why europe can only be the hope for a different future for us if it can be, unlike what it has been so far, a real alternative.
mr president, mr president of the commission, as is fitting in an age of budgets, in addition to being pleased with the progress made, it is also worth highlighting the objectives which have not been achieved or have not exactly been achieved, but in any case in a constructive role for the future. first of all: the introduction of the euro was an important milestone. however, the commission could have done more to support member states in combating the resulting price increases and to help businesses coping with a too strong euro. secondly: the problems of enlargement are far from being solved. the new countries complain that they are still classed as b countries, while the old ones will have to cope with less available funding and a labour force in the enlargement countries that is much cheaper than their own. finally, the still unfinished reform of the stability pact, which has been considered urgent since 2001, especially after the post-9/11 crisis, should be deplored. i would like to remind you, as other members have done, that in this house, and more correctly in the strasbourg chamber, it was president prodi who rightly called the pact stupid. we expect the future commission to give concrete and rapid answers to these problems as well, shortening the time taken by the european bureaucracy as much as possible. in conclusion, mr president prodi, we sincerely regret, perhaps because in 1999 we also gave our confidence, that we saw it, sometimes too much, as a protagonist in italian national politics. i think that this is a negative assessment.
mr president, mr prodi, after five difficult years in which the winds often blew in the opposite direction and the sea was stormy, the budget of the prodi commission is marked by great progress and undeniable results. i would like to begin with the historic turning point in the reunification of europe, or of a large part of it, after the years of the cold war. a great success of democracy and stability. i believe that the prodi commission's budget has its most significant political element in the conception of the commission's role, beyond the individual achievements, which would be too easy to enumerate: the european parliament is not a bureaucratic body, but a political body, which has given impetus and momentum to the whole european process. and in the dialogue and cooperation between the commission and parliament, the political core, the engine of the union, which, in a europeanist and federalist vision, which i share, is the only one that can make europe a truly great supranational political reality, has been strengthened. this has sometimes been a source of annoyance to some national governments, but it has undoubtedly represented a great advance for the union, which has found its first sanction in the new principles of the constitutional treaty. europe certainly still faces difficult challenges in building peace, security, in fighting terrorism effectively, not with war, in giving our societies and economies a new impetus along the lines of lisbon. but these are challenges which can only be overcome with stronger institutions, as you said, with a more mr president, mr prodi, the last five years have been marked by changes and sometimes dramatic events: the wars in afghanistan and iraq, 9/11 and international terrorism, which has reached the heart of europe with the madrid massacre. in the face of these challenges, the national states have not always responded with one voice. but the commission under your leadership has kept the helm steady in the direction of an ever larger and ever more united europe. no one can deny that you have achieved historic goals, destined to change the idea of europe and its role on the international scene forever. the commission's action has given it new impetus, credibility and authority. the unification of europe achieved with the accession of ten new countries, the most important enlargement in history, has healed the wound of fifty years of division and constituted the political response to the end of the cold war. the introduction of the euro and its assertion on international markets have increased europe's economic weight in the world and are now fundamental elements for the strengthening of our continent in an age of economic interdependence and globalisation. the neighbourhood policy has helped to stabilise the balkans and europe's borders. finally, the treaty establishing a european constitution is a historic step and for us a new starting point towards the goal of political integration. these objectives would not have been achieved without his leadership, moral integrity and dedication to the cause of the european union. our group therefore thanks you for the vision and courage you have shown over these five years, laying the foundations for a europe that is more united within itself and a leading player in the world. if i had to sum it up in two words, i would have no doubt: mission accomplished.
mr president, mr prodi, ladies and gentlemen, there is a consistent thread in your work and in the work of your commission: the commission has already made a number of proposals. we have always pursued this ambitious objective with you in bringing italy into the euro. thanks to you, mr prodi, we can now say that political europe is closer, but for that very reason i do not think it is useful to repeat the objectives achieved in the political and institutional fields. it is useful, however, to point out what is still lacking in order to have peace as an objective and a means of resolving the political disputes in the world and to achieve as soon as possible a genuine multipolarism in order to put an end to the clash of civilizations, the result of preventive war and terrorism. a europe of social and individual rights, work for all, a modern and efficient welfare state, a multiracial and multicultural society, where the rights of each are the wealth of all. thank you, president prodi, for what you've done. we have been and will continue to be critical on many issues, we have had and will have different views, but we are and will be united for the europe of peace and progress that we want to build. given recent events, we're sure to regret it.
mr president, mr president of the commission, in recent years europe has made great strides and achieved some important goals: the reunification with the eastern countries, the birth of the euro, the signing of the first constitution; mr president, i draw on my experience of the past few years as head of a local authority in southern italy to highlight one of the elements of the prodi commission's indisputable value: the commission's approach to the problem of the environment is based on the principle of subsidiarity. the peripheries appreciated the working methodology based on a permanent dialogue with regions and local authorities, a methodology reiterated by the commission in its communication of december 2003 and also attested to by the call for representatives of local authorities, entitled 'a thousand debates for europe' on the subject of the constitution, launched by mrs prodi, together with mr cox, president emeritus, and the president of the committee of the regions. in recent years, with the white paper on the new system of governance, the commission has tackled the problem of the community's democratic deficit by re-launching the concept of democracy of proximity. with community policies it has helped the weakest european regions to set up institutions for development, and has introduced a culture of planning, evaluation and integration of interventions.

not in any way open the way to the return of the occupied west bank. on the contrary, it could lead, through the intensification of settlements, to the final acquisition by israel of a large part of process from being overwhelmed by violence again. the international community, and the quartet in particular, should draw some conclusions from all this and reflect on this fact. as regards the possibility of resolving the conflict, the geneva negotiators, unlike at camp david, have shown that it is possible to find dignified and mutually agreed solutions on all the controversial points, mr president, three thousand five hundred palestinians dead, more than a thousand israelis: mr solana, you speak of appeal and say you hope that israeli forces will leave gaza. i have been hearing wise words in this parliament for six years, which place responsibility on one or the other of the parties, but palestinians continue to die every day, killed by an occupying army. it's occupied. in fact, the truth is simple: there is no asymmetry, there is a symmetry. we are facing an occupied country and an international law that is being totally rejected. you have to believe sharon when she tells the son of wiston churchill that she's going to crush the palestinians of gaza and the west bank like a pastrami sandwich. dove weisglass is not lying when she clearly states that leaving gaza simply means putting the peace process into mothballs. it's not lies, it's the truth, it's the political line sharon is pursuing. of course, there can be no justification for the criminal acts that palestinian terrorists may commit against israeli citizens. but can we really only appeal? but europe really has no authority to say to israel: "enough with the israeli military occupation"? and it's not about simplicity or ingenuity. that's the truth. i'm tired of our hypocrisy, i'm tired of this way of doing things, where every day houses are destroyed, girls are killed. ayman, 13, who went to school in a backpack and was frozen is a symbol of the barbarity that a military occupation army can be, as is the barbarity, of course, of hamas sending palestinian boys to throw bombs at israeli citizens. i believe that we have the authority and the power, not just to appeal, but to say enough to the israeli military occupation. instead, let's give voice to the palestinians and israelis who are fighting for peace together: there are so many! those are the voices we need to listen to.

i hope that today's debate is only the beginning of fruitful and necessary interinstitutional and inter-committee cooperation on a matter of the utmost importance to the committee on development, which i have the honour - perhaps without much merit - of chairing and which i represent today. the gsp has been a key instrument of the european union's development policy since its inception in 1971. i would also like to emphasize the credit that goes to the european community, because it was the first to put this system into practice since 1971 and it is still today, in fact, the largest donor in terms of trade concessions to developing countries. in examining the commission's communication, we have started from the principle that the main objective of the generalized system of preferences is, and above all must continue to be, the economic and social development of these countries, and this in the spirit of international solidarity which should govern the union's external policies. it is in this context that we welcome the objectives set out in the communication, namely the simplification of the schemes, the concentration of preferences on the developing countries which need them most and, above all, the promotion of sustainable development. we are fully aware that to achieve these objectives the current system must undergo a substantial reform. but we cannot ignore the fact that any change in the present system will have considerable effects in the countries which are now benefiting from the same system. i am not saying that the commission should not be involved in this. that is why the european parliament has wanted to make its voice heard from the very beginning of the reform process, that is to say, to take account of the concerns, voices and points of view of countries which are interested in this aspect. i think that this democratic requirement is all the more necessary and important at a time when, to be honest, the commission has, very regrettably, failed to undertake a proper consultation of the parties concerned before putting forward its proposals, given also that the council accepted the request to undertake a consultation with the european parliament at its last meeting. the views i express today as chairman of the committee on development are therefore preliminary and are also contained in the resolution which all the political groups have agreed to sign. i hope that today's debate will help to clarify matters and, above all, enable the european commission to incorporate some of our requests into the regulation which is expected to be adopted by the college of commissioners on 20 october. i would therefore ask the commission to answer the questions we have asked in our question. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. the graduation mechanism, the functioning of the gsp+ and the reform of the system of rules of origin. the report to the first, while welcoming the simplification of the grading mechanism, the fact that the commission proposes to rely solely on the market share criterion, leaving aside any indicator of development and poverty, actually leaves us almost dismayed. we cannot ignore the fact that these provisions may have very negative consequences for important exporters. without necessarily being opposed, we would therefore like to study in depth with the commission and the council the consequences and the modalities of this proposal in the months to come. as for the new gsp+ system, which we consider to be positive in that it supports sustainable development, we believe it is essential that clear, reliable and at the same time realistic criteria are defined. we would like to see a strong commitment from the commission to provide the necessary technical assistance. finally, i would like to stress that one of the obstacles to the efficient use of the system has been, as we know, the system of rules of origin. we therefore welcome a substantial reform of the system to promote regional enlargement, although we hope that good intentions will be translated into concrete initiatives: the commission has therefore submitted a proposal and also examined the systems applied by other countries. i am aware that i have not covered enough of the complexity of the subject, but that is all the time i have. i am also very much looking forward to hearing my dear colleague barón crespo, chairman of the committee on international trade, and of course my colleagues, as well as the answers - i hope detailed - that the commission and the council will give. i really hope that we can have a fruitful cooperation, that we can have positive results.
mr president, i welcome the commission's objectives of simplifying, stabilising and clarifying the regimes. the european cooperation policy, which aims to eradicate poverty in the world, cannot be limited to development programmes, however dense, extensive and profitable they may be in many cases, but must use all the instruments at its disposal to promote the growth of the least favoured countries and thus facilitate as much as possible the economy and exports of these countries. this must of course be done not only with transparency and full awareness, and therefore after careful analysis of the impact of the reductions in the proposed action plans, but also quickly so that the poor countries can benefit from the benefits as soon as possible. in addition to purely economic factors such as market shares, other criteria such as the level of development and the commitment of the beneficiaries to embark on a democratic path must also be taken into account, and trade in those products which often constitute the economic peculiarity of a given country, such as textiles for sri lanka, bananas for barbados or cocoa for the ivory coast, must be encouraged. but above all this must be done in close coordination with the wto, to avoid bypassing the authority of the countries involved and to avoid carrying out within the commission itself, as has happened in the past, a regulatory work which should instead remain the strict responsibility of the wto and the entire international community.
mr president, the fact that the recent olympic games in athens saw two gold medals being withdrawn for doping, as well as numerous disqualified athletes, shows the persistence of a bad habit that affects amateur athletes and big professionals alike. the phenomenon, which is constantly growing, is not being effectively combated with the necessary severity and this is probably also why an increasing number of athletes are approaching doping substances, in some cases even with the unconscious conviction that they are almost harmless or at least not so dangerous. the economic impact of doping is also very worrying: it is a very large business, and it is obviously also a business for large criminal organizations. we ask the commission whether the community support measures in the fight against doping, which financed 32 pilot projects in 2000-2002, can really be considered as a positive balance sheet in terms of the effectiveness of the action or whether the entire community programme should not be completely reviewed, taking into account that the 10th conference of ministers responsible for sport is meeting today in the council of europe, to promote honesty and transparency in sport and also considering the high educational values that are linked to the performance of sports activities, highlighted in 2004 which was the year of education through sport. we therefore ask the commission whether it should not be more actively involved in the fight against doping and whether, after having financed projects to study the phenomenon, it should not instead finance more effective information and persuasion campaigns, in short, prevention campaigns.

mr president, we are deeply concerned by the excessive speed with which hundreds of non-community nationals have been deported from italy to libya in recent days, and in particular by the lack of transparency in the procedures used. there are concerns that the italian government's action could seriously undermine the fundamental right to seek asylum and the principle of non-refoulement, which prohibits the forced return of a person to a territory where he or she is at risk of suffering serious human rights violations. italy has the possibility of asking for assistance from europe and the international community and all the more reason for its duty to admit refugees seeking asylum on its territory without any discrimination. amnesty international also asked for more information on the current procedures and on compliance with national and international standards. under these rules, asylum seekers are entitled to legal assistance, interpreters, access to the united nations high commissioner for refugees and to non-governmental organisations, all of which the italian government has effectively prevented, in violation of the principles and values of the european community.

in writing. i am voting in favour of the proposal, but i think that the new gsp should contain: • reference to the fight against drugs as an 'integral part' of sustainable development. it is particularly important that the reference to the fight against drugs be linked to the new gsp+ regime. the current beneficiaries of the 'drug scheme' would risk seeing their efforts in the fight for alternative crops wasted if they were no longer able to benefit from the gsp. • explicit reference to the expiry of the multifibre arrangement and its impact on the textile sector. on the basis of this reference it will then be possible to ask for differentiated treatment for textile products in the context of the discussions for the new regulation. • non-exhaustive reference to the sections of the common customs tariff as the basic criterion for the application of graduation. the sections are, in the present wording, cited only by way of example. • reference to the regional cumulation of origin. transparent and effective rules of origin are important to avoid fraudulent use of the system, especially through commercial triangulations. • explicit reference to greater effectiveness of safeguard and anti-fraud measures. finally, we would like the future commission to finally take care of the application of the special clauses for workers and to make efforts, including the use of economic sanctions, to eradicate the scourge of child labour.

mr president, i speak on behalf of the new italian socialist party, represented in this house by myself and by mr gianni de michelis. the mid-term review should be a clear and calm moment for analysis and comparison. in acknowledging the important results achieved, it is also necessary to highlight the areas and fields of action in which community action has been the most lacking and slow, again with reference to the parameters set by the lisbon strategy. we also want to emphasize again, as reformists, the importance of the fact that economic growth and development cannot and must not be at the expense of the social model of european solidarity, which is advanced and can be updated but not affected in its guidelines and principles. we believe that the objective of creating a europe of citizens is a fundamental objective. the tools of communication and interaction with the people must be identified to bring europe closer to europeans, since in the past, unfortunately, the community institutions were too often perceived as something abstract and distant: this is a trend that needs to be reversed.
mr president, i was somewhat surprised to find that in both introductory speeches, when the mid-term review of the lisbon strategy was discussed, the words 'the lisbon strategy' were never mentioned. environment and sustainable development. i am not interested in formal recognition, in ritual tributes, but i wonder, mr verheugen, whether this is the kind of continuity you have alluded to. apart from that, there are three good reasons why this third pillar of the lisbon strategy should still be considered strategic. the first is that a few days ago there was a very important, once very positive, international event: the eu has also been active in the field of energy and climate change, and has been active in the field of energy security. the second is that, incidentally, the environment is precisely the area in which the member states are lagging behind most in the implementation of community policies. there is, however, an even more important reason, related to the topic that rightly concerns - somewhat obsessively - the chanceries of all europe at this moment: the problem of competitiveness. how can the european union regain its global competitiveness if it does not bet on the new technologies? if it does not take a technological leadership in the field of environmental technologies? this is the question i am raising and i hope it will be a defining point in the strategy review process.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by thanking you for your report. i would like to address two points: the first concerns lisbon. the european social model can certainly be combined with competitiveness, cohesion and solidarity, which are not just slogans but must be translated into political action. to this end, we must evaluate the results in concrete terms and not give up our ambitions. the key, i repeat, is to focus on training, education and research to give everyone the tools to grow and to be active citizens, so that social protection does not become welfare. the second concerns the citizens' europe, which is just beginning to take off and which certainly needs a great deal of effort on our part in the field of communication. but if we do not want this to remain a mere statement, we must support it with an adequate budget in our budget and certainly the constitutional treaty can be a very good steering wheel.
mr president, to talk about security problems in europe and to remain silent, as was done in the reports which introduced this debate, about the dangers of islamic terrorism is very serious and shows the point of addiction we have reached, perhaps also thanks to the propaganda of the fairy tale of moderate islam and the influence that the circles of the very powerful islamic lobby, rich in petrodollars, have on european politics, on individual states and on the media. on the other hand, as regards the important and sensitive problem of asylum, i believe that the effort, which may be late, to place the examination of these practices and situations within the territory and also within the political and social competence of third countries - i am thinking of african countries - should be encouraged. perhaps african leaders have a clearer idea of this, telling us about a tendency to use this technique falsely, this subterfuge of false asylum claims, and urging us to open our eyes. however, to involve african countries, aid policy must be changed and made more transparent and more serious towards the people of africa.

the oral question (b6-0019/04) by mrs záborská, on behalf of the committee on women's rights and gender equality, to the commission, on women and poverty is on the agenda.
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. thank you for the engaging discussion. of course, many points would be worth discussing. i want to pick two. one was the link between the european budget, agricultural spending and poverty and women's poverty in the third world. it was said that almost half of the budget would help to increase poverty among women in the third world. i can't leave it like this. the commission has already made a major effort in the world trade organisation negotiations to reduce agricultural subsidies, to reduce export subsidies and to give the third world countries more opportunities. secondly, agricultural spending is of course essential to preventing poverty in rural areas, in the agricultural sector. we all know that poverty and rural areas have something to do with women, too. in this respect, our programmes in the field of the european budget for rural development are particularly important in preventing poverty among women. the next point concerns the lisbon strategy. the commission has asked for concepts and specific proposals. i would remind you that it was the commission which, in the framework of the lisbon strategy, made it possible to formulate this objective, namely to increase the participation of women in the labour force in a relatively short period of time. it was the commission which pointed out that it is not possible to simply set the objective of increasing the female employment rate without taking concrete action to improve childcare. because the only way to achieve this goal is to have more women in the workforce, and as a woman i was very pleased that the heads of state and government at a summit once dealt with the issue of childcare facilities and came to the conclusion that a higher density of care must be achieved. the commission has already taken a number of measures to improve the situation of women in the european union.
the debate is closed.
the oral question (b6-0018/04) by mr karl-heinz florenz, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, to the commission on the environmental impact of high-intensity active sonar in ships is on the agenda.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the devastating effects of global warming, noise and chemical pollution, overfishing and shipping accidents are dramatically degrading our oceans and, inevitably, the whale populations that inhabit them. we now have sufficient scientific evidence to show that high intensity active sonar from ships can be deadly to whales, dolphins and fish. all cetaceans are extremely dependent on hearing for their survival, many species have acute hearing and use sound, not only to locate prey, but also for navigation and communication, often over long distances. the noise pollution caused by sonar devices not only causes physiological damage, but also interferes with the natural behaviour of animals and their normal communication. it masks the calls and disorients the animals, driving them away from their usual habitat, where they feed and breed. last year we voted a resolution on the protection and conservation of the marine environment. this parliament unanimously called for the application of coherent and coordinated measures to halt the decline of biodiversity by 2010 and to promote the sustainable use of the seas, protecting and conserving marine ecosystems. i am not going to remind you, mr president, that these were the political objectives which we committed ourselves to at the last world summit on sustainable development. i therefore believe that it is our duty to adopt measures to preserve the marine world around us, and i therefore call on the commission to respect the deadlines it has set here, to move from studies to concrete proposals.
the commission is supporting this commitment to halt the loss of biodiversity by 2010. the second is that the commission needs good scientific results if it is to make effective recommendations. in this respect, the study is very important. it will be available soon, and you will of course receive the results and suggestions. the third point: i will refer this resolution to my colleague who will be co-responsible for security research in the commission in the future, because i believe it is very important to exploit the possibilities we have in the field of research.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i shall speak for two minutes in support of an important cause. i believe that this is essential if we are to develop a culture which can help prevent and solve the problems that man is causing himself through the endless series of pain, injury and death that too often affect the civilian population and that are caused by the dispersion of landmines in many areas. mines, which are deadly weapons of war and which we must absolutely combat, because they are directed against the civilian population, remain active for an infinite period of time and often make it impossible to carry out effective mine clearance operations. we know that the international community has moved on these issues. the ngos have been active in a major campaign, supported by a collection of signatures, and through a spontaneous movement that has led governments in many countries to conclude the ottawa treaty. a major international treaty that banned anti-personnel mines. today we must go further with that treaty, we must encourage a movement of opinion and then, i hope, action by governments which can draw up further conventions, acts and protocols in order to ban other types of mines. these very deadly mines, cluster mines, which have a devastating effect many times greater than that of anti-personnel mines. anyone who has seen these findings, collected in some horror museum, if i may say so, can only feel indignation and a strong incentive to commit themselves in this direction. we were recently in slovenia, and we were able to see in a museum the effects of this type of mine. we could see pictures, images of camps, images of people injured. meet the people who are bearing the effects of these tools, which the international community must ban. they are not traditional weapons of war, precisely because they come from the battlefield and go to the civilian population, women and children. those children playing in the fields, those women going to the cultivated fields to gather their nourishment, and where instead they find death. i hope that this parliament will commit itself to extending the existing convention to include this other type of lethal instrument.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, war is becoming a means of world government today. the european parliament has a duty to protect the rights of the peoples of europe. the request in the joint resolution which we are voting on today introduces this clause and calls for a moratorium, but also a commitment, to the abolition of the use, production, stockpiling and export of cluster munitions. ammunition dangerous to the civilian population. women, children, old people, men, whom i have seen with their flesh torn and their limbs amputated -- in kabul, in kandahar, in pristina -- in clinics trying to save the salvageable. barbarism, because these bombs are used by our democratic countries. the united states dropped thousands of cluster bombs on the territory of afghanistan and iraq. around kabul i saw these yellow objects that attract children and they are the same color as the food rations, which always came from the sky to the villages. at least 57 countries have stockpiles of cluster munitions, 22 of which are in europe. there is no doubt that there is a violation of the provisions of international law. we must put human rights above military gain: the right to life for millions of people. that is a goal we must take on board. this must be a commitment for the european parliament, as it was for the ratification of the ottawa mine ban treaty, thanks to the tireless work of thousands of people in non-governmental organizations. in europe, we still have to convince poland, finland and latvia to join. we must also negotiate a new protocol to the convention on conventional weapons and promote, at the conference to review the ottawa treaty, a consensus among the states to stop anti-tank mines with sensitive implants, which explode upon human contact. a resolution is not enough, we cannot stop, and for this reason we have also asked for permanent monitoring by the european parliament.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i think it is a good thing that the european parliament should work to ensure that freedom and justice prevail not only in europe - since there are also some countries on our continent where, unfortunately, there is still a lack of freedom - but throughout the world. i think that is one of our tasks, one of our main goals. i therefore believe that we should be particularly concerned about those areas where the risks of the most basic human rights being violated are greatest. the european union has a duty to help the people of the middle east, and to help the people of the middle east. however, i would add that there have been reports recently which, on more than one occasion, have been completely contradictory. this gives us a picture of the present situation which does not give us definitive certainty. i say definitive as we already have some elements. in recent days, the iranian authorities have claimed that what is written in our documents is not true. i cannot, of course, agree with you on this point, but it seems likely that some inaccuracies have been made. i do not think that we should take what the iranian authorities tell us as a given, because we know that in the past, and perhaps even now, this country has not been particularly transparent and democratic. however, perhaps not exactly in accordance with the rules of this house, i think it would be prudent at this time to propose a postponement of the vote on this resolution. i would like to ask the commissioner to give us a brief explanation of his position. i do not, however, wish to question the claims of the iranian embassy. in fact, the iranian embassy sent a letter to our president borrell yesterday - and i have a copy of it - which follows on from an epistolary correspondence also with mrs svensson, in which she disputes the content of our resolutions. i understand that it is not yet clear whether minors are still being executed in iran. according to the iranian authorities, it appears that all executions of minors have been suspended in the past two years. so, having said that, i would suggest that we postpone the vote until another date, and in the meantime listen to the iranian authorities again to get more precise ideas and to consider our vote with more calm and serenity. in any case, if we go to the vote, the uen group will of course vote in favour of this resolution, and i shall abstain in the light of what i have just said.

on behalf of the group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, there is a danger that this debate will become an annual deadline, where we all agree more or less, but then there is only a list of good intentions and appeals to the commission and the council. to avoid this, it is essential that the code of conduct becomes a legally binding instrument and that clear penalties are identified against companies based in the european union that do not comply with it. the union must also commit itself to asking other countries, such as the united states, russia and china, to join the code of conduct - also in view of the fact that more than 50% of the world's arms production takes place in the member countries of the un security council. as we speak, my country, italy, has increased its arms exports by 25% year on year and is forecasting an increase of 40% in the future. among the biggest beneficiaries of italy's arms sales is saudi arabia, which is certainly not an example of a country that respects rights. i believe that we must absolutely maintain the arms embargo on china, and furthermore that the defence of human rights must absolutely take precedence over the economic interests of profit. one last note: military spending last year worldwide was $900 billion, of which $700 billion was in the civilised countries of the first world alone. this is not the civilization we want!
mr president, it may also be true that the embargo on china seems anachronistic, especially after the lifting of the embargo on libya, which is certainly not a model of respect for human rights. it is also understandable that china, as a sovereign state, would seek to strengthen its defence to safeguard its security and territorial integrity. it is also true that some small progress has been made in improving respect for human rights, that china remains a leading player in the fight against terrorism and is exerting useful pressure on north korea to stop developing nuclear weapons. however, it is also true that beijing is trying to exploit the differences between us and the united states on this issue. i am surprised that no one in this house has stressed that point: the transatlantic relations, which must remain our paramount concern, will be affected by the more or less common attitude we adopt towards the rising power of china. the strategic balance, however precarious, currently in place in the taiwan strait must also be taken into account. taiwan, which is a model of democracy that china is still very far away from. so if for realpolitik reasons, economic and political reasons, rather than respect for democracy, we want to abolish the embargo, we can only do so by committing the people's republic of china to clear and continuous progress in respecting human rights, as the commission representative has said today. we are trying to discuss, i do not mean to agree, our position with that of the united states and those european countries which are currently opposed to lifting the embargo. i think it is essential to have as much common ground and agreement as possible on this matter.

the next item is the commission statement on the situation in cuba.
on behalf of the group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, our friendship with cuba is longstanding and enduring. you see, cuba represents the attempt of a small island to decide its own existence, and to do it differently than the great imperial power. we look at this with solidarity. of course, we can also express our criticisms explicitly and without any qualms, as we have done and are doing, for example, with regard to the death penalty or certain judicial procedures. we do not want to see the european union's military action against cuba. cuba has been suffering for decades from a ferocious economic blockade by the us administration. i do not see why europe should accept such behaviour. on the contrary, it is relations, exchanges and comparisons that can foster understanding and improvement. in latin america, important, historic things are happening, as evidenced by the progressive victories in countries such as brazil, venezuela, uruguay. the logic of liberal globalisation and war - we have seen terrible images today - calls for a different policy: cuba is part of this latin america. this latin america needs a relationship with europe and europe needs a relationship with latin america. we call for the repeal of all acts of discrimination against cuba, and therefore the council's common position must be followed by a similar request to the us government. at the same time we call for the strengthening of free and fruitful relations.
i speak on behalf of the new italian socialist party. the council unanimously adopted political sanctions against the cuban regime in july 2003 following the indictment of 75 dissidents and the death sentences of three citizens who tried to flee to the usa; the fact that the cuban authorities have not yet taken action against the cuban authorities is a fact which, unfortunately, is part of a general picture of the continuing and repeated violations and denials of fundamental freedoms in cuba, as was recently confirmed by colat. the regime of fidel, in deep trouble, as a result of increasing internal and international pressure, is becoming more and more oppressive and repressive, especially towards the new generations, who dream of a future of freedom, true democracy and development, a new and open horizon, incompatible with the current communist establishment. europe is therefore continuing firmly on the road it has taken, with particular attention, however, to avoiding any further negative repercussions for the population, in order to lay the foundations for a fruitful and renewed dialogue without resentment between the cuban people and europe in the post-castro era. friends of cuba, friends of cubans, enemies of all dictators!
mr president, there is little time to deal with a matter as complex and rich as the cuban one. certainly in the short run we must remember david's battle against goliath. the battle of an island that, for over forty years, has been waged by the giant of the world: the united states. but we are in europe, and we must talk about europe. i think it is right that the european union should change its attitude towards cuba, because the current position is entirely dependent on pressure from the united states government. europe would thus also be signalling its independence, its autonomy - which does not mean confrontation with the united states - but it does mean autonomy and independence in the judgement of international affairs. we are talking about fighting terrorism, about fighting for human rights, but can we ask a question? why is it that anywhere in the world, if someone hijacks a ship or a plane, they are called terrorists, while if they do the same thing in cuba, they are called freedom fighters or dissidents? why are there two weights and measures when it comes to cuba and when it comes to iraq? these are the reasons why - while we are romanticists and love poetry - we would not hesitate to support all the acts and resolutions in favour of cuba, its people and its original revolution.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, cuba has been in a state of waiting for some time. pressure from outside is mounting, while there is a relative deterioration of freedom of expression and human rights in general at home. in this situation, the european union must take a clear position without waiting for further developments. what is the prospect for europe? it is right to ask this, because there are those who wish, particularly in the united states, for a traumatic collapse of the regime within a hegemonic vision, essentially unchanged since the imposition of the embargo until today. at the same time, i must say that it is not clear what kind of dynamic vision of the future of their country the cuban ruling classes have today after the end of the cold war. all in all, we are facing a deadlock, caused by political stagnation both at home and abroad. in this situation, i believe that the union cannot simply retreat behind the issue of human rights, or worse still, accommodate those who use them as a political tool. it could be shown, by many examples, that what is true of cuba is not equally true of many other countries. the recent eu-asean summit, with full participation of burma, is a good example of the double standards in human rights. in this house today, commissioner patten said that the commission has never made a direct link between the possible lifting of the arms embargo and human rights policy. the european parliament has been rightly pointing out that further progress in the field of human rights could have facilitated a review of the european attitude. - i know. i do not see why the same pragmatic approach, based on a positive approach of incentives - and not simply sanctions and punishments - should not be applied to cuba. in the era of globalisation, helping to break cuba's isolation by means of closer economic cooperation would, as we know and as experience has shown, have positive repercussions on its internal policy, starting with human rights. only those who look to the future with the eyes of the past, with a taste for ideological revenge, can ignore this simple truth. the european union therefore needs a change of pace. we need more imagination and we need a new and independent policy towards cuba. a more advanced and closer phase of political dialogue and cooperation must be opened, fully involving not only the government authorities but also civil society in all its components. we must open the way to a peaceful and agreed transition towards a fully democratic and pluralist evolution, and affirm the idea of a cuban path to democracy. these must now be the objectives of the european union.
i have received four motions for resolutions at the end of the debate. the motion for a resolution: i have to say that i am not in favour of the amendment. the vote will take place on wednesday 17 november 2004 at 12.30 p.m.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i appreciate that president balkenende has taken as his starting point the dramatic events in the netherlands, which have disturbed tolerant holland. the netherlands of integration, although that word was completely absent from the president's speech today. however, i was not at all pleased that the very serious events in iraq were not mentioned today. yesterday, devastating images were echoed in our homes - us marines killing wounded iraqis - as if barbarity and horror were ordinary administration that should not even be mentioned. i believe that europe cannot stand by passively while these events are taking place, or deal only with the reconstruction appropriations - as the council has done recently - or simply meet with allawi, or rely on the forthcoming elections as the event which will resolve the conflict. on the hague programme: i do not think there is any continuity with tampere, but i do see a qualitative leap that was caused by a misunderstanding. we cannot put the issue of organised crime, terrorism, human trafficking and immigration in one pot, as if they were one problem. in reality, this is a scheme to limit europe's action to the field of security, to the exclusion of justice and freedom. i believe that islamic terrorism must be fought through intercultural and interreligious dialogue. unlike his deputy minister, president balkenende, who declared war on islam the day after van gogh's murder, or like his country does by funding religious schools. i think instead that we should build a school where catholics, muslims, protestants and atheists all live together. finally, human trafficking and immigration are not the same thing. we must punish the criminals, but above all satisfy the needs: the need for refugees from war and hunger, who come to our territory, seeking shelter in our solidarity and integrated europe.
mr president, we are very concerned about the worsening situation in iraq. the attack in falluja and the way in which the civilian population was involved, in addition to being yet another denial of international law and the geneva convention, are fuelling the spiral of hatred. i would like to turn my thoughts from here to poor mrs margaret assan, who was brutally murdered yesterday. both terrorism and war as a response to terrorism are pushing away the prospect of peace and democratization in the country. we therefore call on the union to play a role in the conference to be held in sharm el sheik on 23 november and to use its influence, in the light of the unity achieved, to demand full acceptance by all of un resolution 1546, starting with respect for international law and human rights. in these circumstances, it is legitimate to have serious doubts about the real possibility of holding elections by january 2005. the outcome of the elections in the united states and the replacement of colin powell could further embolden the ultra-conservative forces, both in iraq and in the middle east. i am less optimistic about the middle east, because it does not appear that prime minister sharon has given more to abu mazen during his presidency than he has given to arafat. in this connection, in addition to renewing our solidarity with the palestinian people for the loss of their historic leader, we call for the union to commit itself by political and financial means to the proper conduct of the presidential and local elections in palestine and to bring the road map out of the current deadlock. this would make it possible to replace, i repeat, to replace the unilateral acts of the sharon government with genuine negotiations, which, by resolving the most controversial points, such as the settlements and the right of return, would lead to the creation of a palestinian state and the coexistence of the two peoples.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the decisions of the european council come at a delicate juncture in the institutional life of the union, on the eve of the historic signing of the constitutional treaty in rome and in the wake of the new interinstitutional debate that has arisen as a result of the failure of the european parliament to vote on the investiture of the new commission. the text of the council conclusions finds a parliament more aware of the new era of parliamentary democracy that has opened up in europe and deserves some reflection. first of all, the lisbon objectives must be pursued with more impetus, rigor and the courage to choose, as kok argues. the challenge of global competition requires us to keep up our ambitions and requires strong political responsibility and determination to complete structural reforms by governments and support the change in the dimension of social security. but a balanced budget is needed if the lisbon strategy is to remain a key policy component of the commission, as president romano prodi has argued. this is also important in the preparation of the fundamental european pact for youth, whose essential coordinating elements are education, training, work and research. these issues require smart, generous and coordinated policies, but above all, certainty of investment. finally, the strategy for communicating europe and making the constitutional treaty known also requires investment, but to date i do not think there is a suitable expenditure line in the budget.

the next item is the report (a6-0030/2004) by mr proinsas de rossa, on behalf of the committee on petitions, on the 2003 annual report of the european ombudsman.
on behalf of the group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would like to congratulate mr de rossa on his effective and complete report. the charter of fundamental rights of the european union in nice has further defined the content of european citizenship, which is already included in the maastricht treaty. the protection of the fundamental rights and freedoms of the european citizen has thus taken a step forward in improving relations between the union and its citizens. although citizens are entitled to community rights and rules concerning their activities, they have little chance of defending themselves against possible cases of maladministration. the need to ensure civil coexistence and to respond concretely to the needs of the community is therefore becoming more and more urgent. the ombudsman's task is to propose appropriate solutions and to assert the fundamental rights and guarantees of the individual. the institution of the ombudsman is the most visible expression of that sovereignty which is rooted in the people and which works to defend individual rights against the institutions, which citizens ask to be protected. in essence, the european ombudsman and the integrated european civil defence system offer every citizen the possibility of making use of them and, if necessary, of making the european institutions more transparent and effective, of contributing to the enlargement of the union to include the other candidate countries, and of promoting the development of a culture of protection of citizens' rights. the european ombudsman is therefore the terminus of any citizen who feels that his or her rights have been infringed: the european parliament, which is the highest authority in the community, can investigate and propose solutions in cases of maladministration by the community institutions and, if the conciliation solution proposed by it fails, can make recommendations or refer the matter to the european parliament. according to the annual report which our ombudsman has presented to us today, he has dealt with a wide range of issues, including the practical contribution of the european ombudsman network: the commission has also been active in the field of the protection of the environment, and has been involved in the development of the european environment agency (eea) since its inception in 1995. the importance of his timely and impartial action is therefore plain to all. its role, introduced into the community legal order by the maastricht treaty of 1992, is now mainly in the field of defence of citizens against a jungle of rules and structures, already extensive within individual countries, which will become increasingly complex in the construction of a more complete supranational legal order. the european parliament, therefore, in thanking the ombudsman, must commit itself to making the ombudsman and the committee on petitions, which is its operational arm for analysis, monitoring and proposals, more valuable, in order to promote the process of democratization and social cohesion. the european ombudsman's work, supported by the commission and its technical structures, makes it possible today to consolidate the concept of european citizenship, to spread and enhance the culture of civil defence in europe, and to move from a model of bureaucracy that weighs on the union's institutions to that of civil defence, and above all to that of the power of citizens, which is complete democracy. in view of this qualitative leap, i welcome the report presented today on the organisational side. in this way we can continue to strengthen the system of protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms, as well as the principles of cooperation and subsidiarity, so that the european citizen becomes effectively the dominus and not a mere voter called upon to vote.
mr president, i speak on behalf of the new italian socialist party. i am the mayor of a town on the outskirts of rome and in my administrative work i have had the opportunity to highlight the importance of the ombudsman's role as an interface and filter between the administration and the citizen. the european ombudsman, mutatis mutandis, must - or should - represent a modern, effective and efficient point of interaction between the community institutions and the europeans, bringing us closer to the realization of the fundamental right of every citizen to good administration carried out on the basis of transparent and fair criteria, as enshrined in the charter of nice. the way forward is to be welcomed, with the establishment of a network of ombudsmen and other national and local bodies to which complaints for which the ombudsman is not competent can be referred. efforts must be stepped up to create a comprehensive and effective system of out-of-court redress for the benefit of european citizens. even after these first months of work as a member of the committee on petitions, i must nevertheless consider that, as the data contained in the present report, which has been so well drafted by mr de rossa, show, there is still a great deal of misinformation and confusion about the exact powers of the european ombudsman. although the work carried out in 2003 was regular and in accordance with the procedures, it is important to make a significant difference in the perception of individual european citizens by bringing the ombudsman, who has been too detached, closer to everyday life. the desired revision of the ombudsman's statute will have to focus on this aspect, increasing the real and concrete possibilities of solving the problems raised. a european ombudsman who becomes a real link between the community, the police and the institutions which govern it.

the next item is the commission statement on world aids day.
on behalf of the group. the scenario that has been illustrated here is not something that depends solely on a natural catastrophe but represents the dramatic synthesis between the aggressiveness of a virus and the unbridled pursuit of economic profit by a powerful few such as pharmaceutical multinationals. while 95% of the world's hiv-positive people are currently unable to access treatment, the situation is set to deteriorate further from 1 january 2005, when the derogation from article 31 of the trips agreement will end. from that point on, india, with its one billion people and other developing countries, will no longer be able to produce the drugs. already now those who produce them cannot sell them to other developing countries and african countries. it is therefore necessary that, beyond words and declarations of principle, the european union should first of all endeavour to obtain a further derogation from the trips agreement, pending its complete amendment. not only that: the european union must oppose the us practice of blackmailing developing countries in bilateral agreements to prevent them from exercising the right, laid down in the doha declarations, to use or import generic medicines. we must support the global fund for aids by increasing the funds allocated, and i propose that the european union at least raise the figure to one billion euros and that everyone, including italy, honour the commitments that have been made. according to fortune, the average salary of the top nine pharmaceutical executives in the world is $42 million a year. in the face of this, tens of millions of people die without treatment. in the face of these injustices, all forms of moralism must be overcome. we have to remember that prophylaxis is something useful: a medical device that should be given away absolutely free of charge. it's one of the tools we need to beat the virus.
the unaids report on the evolution of the epidemic was even more distressing this year than in the past: the projections for sub-saharan africa and its survival in the new century are dire. this tragedy is added to other equally serious human tragedies, such as the exploitation of adult and child prostitution, drug addiction, poor sanitation, the breakdown of families and the increase in young orphans, as well as infant mortality, which reaches impressive figures: 500,000 children die every year worldwide, which means 1,300 children a day from aids. that is why it is important today for the european parliament, united without distinction of party or nationality, to make a strong appeal to all the member states of the european union, starting with my own country - italy - not to throw away the efforts made so far and to continue to support the action of the global fund to fight aids, tuberculosis and malaria. let us avoid reductionist thinking in the fight against aids, not forgetting that this virus is not only sexually transmitted but that there are many other causes of its spread: the infected syringe, breast-feeding and lack of adequate medical monitoring. let us not forget that the condom is merely a means of containment - useful, of course, but of containment - and not of prevention of the virus. if we really want to fight this disease, we need to address it at its social, economic and ethical roots, by raising the standards of well-being, prevention and treatment in the countries most affected.
the commission. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i have listened very carefully to the issues raised and the views expressed during this debate. i must say that it is a real pleasure and encouragement to hear that the honourable members not only share my deep concern about the issue of hiv/aids but also my firm conviction that we must contribute fully and effectively to alleviating the terrible suffering caused by this epidemic, both in the rest of the world and in the european union. we must be particularly aware of the seriousness of this problem today. but let me comment on some of the issues raised by the honourable members. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. it has already been mentioned by the honourable members, it is no longer a matter which concerns only vulnerable groups. it is something that concerns each of us, and that is why the way it is currently spreading within families, has affected families, children, not only in the third world but also in the european union itself. i would like to assure you that the whole european commission, all our colleagues, are aware of this matter. we share the same concerns and have set the same objectives, and i want to assure you that there will be full coordination with all colleagues in combating this problem, in drawing up and implementing programmes in this direction. as regards research, i would like to point out that, in the case of the vaccine, hiv vaccine research is a priority under the sixth framework programme for research and development and it can be expected that up to eur 130 million will be allocated to hiv vaccine research. as regards medicines, i would like to remind you that i fully agree that there should be access to cheap medicines for all those who have a problem with this disease, particularly, of course, for poor countries and for poor groups of the population who cannot afford these high treatment costs. i would also like to remind you that the commission has recently - and it is also mentioned in your resolution - adopted a proposal for compulsory licensing of medicines for export to countries in need. this point is now at the stage of co-decision and i would hope that, judging from other cases in the past on very sensitive issues, there will be speed and diligence to complete this proposal as soon as possible and that within this proposal there will be the possibility of accessing precisely the cheap medicines. there is a great need - and i repeat this - to lift the social stigma. one of the big problems we face today in terms of the spread of this disease is that because of this pressure, many people are infected or have been infected with the virus and they don't even know it. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work, and on the draft directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to carcinogens at work, and on the draft directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to carcinogens at work. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat this scourge, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for his report. i am glad and i would like to thank parliament and the european commission for the active interest we are seeing and we want to believe that in this fight we will be close allies in a meaningful way so that there is really an effective response to this problem. there is no room for complacency, we must continue with the same intense effort, show the same zeal to prevent and stop the spread of this disease but, at the same time, contribute to the relief of those who are already infected, as well as to their treatment. i look forward to a future constructive dialogue between the commission and parliament in pursuit of our common objectives.

the internet represents a universe of freedom without boundaries, but, being without rules governing its use, it can also represent a universe of infinite freedom of abuse, conditioning and violation. i have been conducting political action in the european parliament for years, presenting dozens of motions for resolutions and questions, press releases and statements in order to establish democratic rules, that is to say rules and laws that protect the citizen and that affect those who use the network for criminal purposes. this year i succeeded in getting the daphne programme to include in its objectives the request to 'study the possibility of creating filters to prohibit the dissemination of child pornography on the internet'. now, finally, the council and the european parliament have decided to set up a new programme to promote safer use of the internet. the commission is also proposing to increase the number of national authorities responsible for the protection of the internet. that is why i recall that in italy i initiated a pilot case against a website which disseminates false information about members of the european parliament and about europe. there is still no case law on this subject, that is to say, it is not known how to prevent a national of one member state from spreading false information about a national of another member state. freedom, to be such, needs rules!
