mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i speak on behalf of the new italian socialist party, which is in favour of the eurlings report and therefore of the opening of negotiations with turkey. turkey's accession would confirm the nature of the union as an open and tolerant society, which draws strength from its diversity and is held together by the shared values of freedom, democracy, the rule of law and respect for human rights. moreover, by providing an alternative model to the closed and sectarian society advocated by radical islamists, europe could play an invaluable role in future relations between the west and the islamic world. more generally, there is no doubt that at a time when europe is committed to taking on greater responsibilities in world politics, turkey's accession would considerably strengthen the union's capacity to become a major player on the international political scene, especially in hot spots such as the middle east, the black sea basin, the south caucasus and central asia. in addition, as confirmed by the oecd, turkey's accession could add value to europe's economic weight in the world, thanks to its vast geographical size, its vast natural resources and its young and highly skilled workforce. the opening of accession negotiations will be both a starting and a finishing point. it will be a turning point, since turkey's first application to join the then european economic community dates back to 1959. the country has modernized in recent years, achieving important results through far-reaching reforms and innovations, especially in the last two years. however, the opening of negotiations will also be a starting point, since the negotiations, which may begin as soon as turkey meets the political criteria of copenhagen, will have a duration and an outcome which will depend on progress made, particularly with regard to the economy and the acquis communautaire. the european union and turkey therefore appear to be united by a major challenge to jointly lay the foundations for the construction of the europe of tomorrow.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, should we open negotiations with turkey? the answer to this question is neither simple nor obvious. on the one hand, there is the legitimate expectation of turkey to join the european union. turkey's efforts to bring its legislation and institutions closer to european standards, its stubborn political will and its strong conviction that it is an active player in the process of european enlargement, cannot be ignored or underestimated. however, as europeans, we have a duty not to make concessions, because democracy cannot afford it, nor can it sell off values, human rights or civil liberties. on these latter issues, much remains to be done in turkey, particularly with regard to respect for women and children, the protection of minorities and the abolition of torture and the death penalty. finally, there are two knots to be untied, two inescapable premises for turkey: the european union is committed to the restoration of normal relations with armenia, by reopening the borders, and with cyprus. in the latter case, the withdrawal of troops is a preliminary step to accession, as is the recognition of the cypriot state. it is unacceptable that turkey, at the time of its application for membership of the european union, not only does not recognise a member state such as cyprus but also maintains thousands of soldiers on cypriot soil. turkey will therefore have to face a long process which necessarily excludes a priori accession. however, realistically we cannot close the door on a muslim country with secular institutions, which is seeking a european path of modernisation and development. the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i hope that the european parliament will send a clear message to the council on 17 december, namely that it should open negotiations with turkey with a view to accession. this is the right and consistent response to the great progress made by this country, as the commission's rigorous and thorough report shows. of course, the opening of negotiations will involve long delays and further profound changes, both on the part of turkey and the european union itself. at the moment, this seems to me to be the only viable option. i hear talk of special partnerships which should replace the prospect of accession in the event that turkey is unable to absorb the acquis communautaire. opening negotiations does not automatically guarantee membership of the union, but talking about other prospects right now gives our interlocutors a reasonable suspicion of the true intentions of the twenty-five. let us therefore try to be clear and transparent and give this country a real chance. the political will of the present and future leaders, and above all of the turkish people, will determine the prospects for our relations. we do not give an alibi to the opponents of democracy and secularism in that country, because we may bitterly regret it. i would like to say to mr camre that my country has had honour killing and the abolition of the crime of rape by marriage in its legislation, but i would never have dreamed of saying that this was due to catholic culture. i therefore invite you to reflect on this point.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i hope that the council will set a precise date for the opening of negotiations, in the knowledge that from that date a long process will begin, the outcome of which will depend very much on the further changes that will be brought about in turkey during that period. we must reject all forms of racism by those who seek to turn religion into an instrument of discrimination. the coexistence of religions and cultures is the only future for a democratic and multiethnic europe. the negotiations with turkey must focus on the solution of the cyprus problem, full respect for human rights, political recognition of the conflict in kurdistan, and a commitment to a peaceful solution and an immediate, of course bilateral, cessation of hostilities. it is precisely the kurdish democratic organizations and human rights associations in turkey, which still suffer daily or are witnesses to profound injustices, that are asking us to open negotiations so that they can involve the whole of europe in their fight for democracy and respect for human rights. the amendments to the constitution and the penal code that have been made so far are undoubtedly insufficient. much remains to be done, such as reducing the power of the army, which must be subordinated to the role of the executive and legislative branches, amending the electoral law and allowing the use of the kurdish language in official acts.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would just like to say that turkey should not join the european union because turkey is not in europe!
i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. the eurlings report is a rarity in this house. it is the rare record of violations of human rights, minority rights, international rights in a state. the question of torture, religious freedom, women's rights, the constitution, the question of the occupation of part of the european union or of a member country of the european union, the question of the armenians, freedom of expression, are among the most important. the wrong consequence of this list of violations is, in my view, the opening of negotiations, as many colleagues have said. the negotiations with slovakia were interrupted a few years ago and today compliance with the copenhagen criteria is an option for the future without a clear position being taken. the democratization efforts of turkey must be supported, but this certainly does not require membership of the european union, it requires a policy of good neighbourliness, it requires a policy of good partnership. two thirds of the european population are against the opening of accession negotiations and against turkey's accession to the european union. in some countries, such as germany, france and austria, over 70% are against it. the question arises as to why the commission, many members, why the council, why many countries of the council are pursuing a policy which is contrary to the clearly defined interests and wishes of the people. this is politically incomprehensible and extremely shortsighted, because it puts the opinion and the prospects of the european union in great difficulty. i am in favour of a privileged partnership and not of turkey's membership of the european union.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, what we are discussing today in this house, and what we have been discussing for years in the european parliament in official meetings and in private discussions, is a difficult and complex problem. we in forza italia have weighed the pros and cons and have come to the firm and convincing conclusion that it is useful and essential to start accession negotiations. to refuse them would be to frustrate turkey's efforts to modernize and develop a democratic regime, to hand it over to the confusion and despotism of unstable regions such as the middle east and the caucasus, and to send a negative signal to the whole of the moderate islamic world, reinforcing the intransigence of the fundamentalists and empowering those who preach the clash of civilizations. today we have an opportunity to bridge the gap between islam and the west, thanks to a country that has roots in both the history of christianity and islam. by opening the doors of the european union to turkey, we could send a strong signal to those muslim countries which have embarked, despite great difficulties, on the road to democracy and freedom, to convince them that this is the right way, a signal that the european union does not consider islam and democracy to be incompatible. i would remind those who are sceptical and opposed to turkey that by opening negotiations with turkey we will greatly increase our security against terrorism, which is a threat we will have to face for many years to come. it is for these reasons that we shall vote in favour of mr eurlings' report, to which we express our sincere congratulations for the enormous work carried out in conditions of particular difficulty and for having been able to find useful compromises between the very different tendencies which animate this parliament.
on behalf of the group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i am very pleased that during today's debate many colleagues from various political groups, some of them unknown, have spoken at length about sustainability. i think this is important, because i am sure that this issue will become a real priority for the commission as well. at a time when many citizens are increasingly distrustful of the european project, the first strategic objective of the new commission and all the other community institutions should be to return to being visibly effective in improving the quality of life of citizens, to become a positive instrument for democratic growth inside and outside the european union and to transform the union into an area of technological innovation geared to ecological and social sustainability. we believe that three commitments are necessary to this end. the first commitment concerns consistency with the objectives set and the ability to enforce them. we therefore strongly urge the commission to be more determined to take into account the key commitments of the european union, such as sustainability, social cohesion and gender equality in all its policy proposals, and to make a concrete commitment to re-establish a balance between the three pillars of the lisbon agenda - economic, social and environmental - in order to avoid promoting rigid economic objectives at the expense of the quality of life of our citizens individually. we call on the commission to put forward bold proposals for a coordinated effort to revive investment in environmental technologies and employment measures. as regards competitiveness, we would like the indicators to be extended to include qualitative, social and environmental indicators such as income distribution, poverty and energy efficiency. we believe that consistency is also measured by the ability to enforce the commitments made. we know that many countries are not in line with the objectives of the kyoto protocol and we therefore believe that the ways in which the commission will help these countries to comply with the kyoto protocol will be an important element of consistency. in this connection, we would like to revive our old proposal for a climate stability pact, which provides for a system of joint management of the kyoto commitments that is not only repressive but also positive. the second commitment we must absolutely reassess is the courage to reject easy conformity which ignores the european union's responsibility in the world and makes it a reference point and a model for those who, in the rest of the world, are fighting for the defence of human rights and freedom. this is a very important point for us. we cannot, for reasons of trade and economy, refrain from expressing our views on human rights in china and russia and the war in chechnya. we shall shortly be discussing the dayton agreements, which are celebrating their tenth anniversary. we must also act on this point and we hope that the commission will be able to take positive initiatives. the third and final commitment concerns the capacity to represent the interests of the european union as such, not being overly influenced by the balance of power between individual states and the council in general, and finding genuine alliances between those in the european parliament, in the associative world and in the productive world who share the idea that the european union is much more than the sum of the union's governments. we therefore call on the barroso commission to reconsider and possibly withdraw the bolkestein directive, to defend reach with its teeth and nails, to propose a new directive on services of general interest, to abandon or at least reconsider the policy of the previous commission on gmos and to open the way to legal immigration with courage and concrete action. mr president, i would like to conclude by mentioning at least four fundamental points of the interinstitutional agreement which we would like to conclude with you. the first is certainly the code of conduct for commissioners and a serious approach to the issue of conflict of interest. the second concerns the question of impact assessment. the evaluation of new laws cannot be arbitrary and must be based on uniformly applied criteria. the european parliament is running behind on this point. we know that businesses and the economy in general are much more active. we must encourage the commission and parliament to evaluate the laws that are passed also in terms of sustainability and social impact. the third point concerns the reopening of the access to documents dossier. we are extremely dissatisfied with the present situation and we sincerely hope that in the coming years we will be able to achieve positive results. the fourth and final point concerns a very complex subject, which we shall certainly have occasion to discuss, namely the commission's attitude to violations of community law by member states. we are very concerned about the accumulated delays and the fact that the commission seems to have practically abandoned this priority, especially in the environmental field. we really hope that in the future the commission will reconsider this priority, because the credibility of the european union also depends on its ability to enforce the law.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i am convinced that the new commission should also have as one of its priorities the initiation of a constructive debate on the stability pact and its possible reform. let's clear the air of any misunderstandings. no one thinks, and the commissioner responsible has done well to emphasize this strongly, that he can imagine a reform to circumvent the maastricht criteria. the strict observance of the treaty and the commitment to the gradual reduction of debt remain the necessary objectives of an economically credible and solid europe. however, i think we can think about the operational criteria on the basis of which the debt reduction objective can be achieved, and think not so much of reform as of a transformation of the pact, which can reconcile budgetary rigor and flexibility in order to boost growth, development and employment and also, finally, the lisbon strategy. the italian government has made its own reflection which has not gone unheard. in the face of strong concern about the overvaluation of the euro and the loss of europe's competitiveness vis-à-vis the us and emerging countries, the proposal could be to deduct from the calculation of state budgets investments in infrastructure and research. in other words, it is a question of introducing the golden rule to achieve the objective of fiscal consolidation without jeopardizing the recovery. the pact, in conclusion, can no longer be content with being just a pact for stability, and unfortunately also a pact for economic stagnation, but must become a pact for competitiveness, for structural reforms and above all a pact to guarantee a future for our young people.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, mr president of the commission, with the lisbon strategy, among other things, europe has set itself the ambitious goal of becoming the most advanced knowledge society in the world. however, such an objective cannot be achieved without a revival of basic research and innovation, which in turn requires strengthening european material capacities, infrastructures and centres of excellence and aiming at improving human capital, with the entry of thousands of young researchers. this change of course requires an increase in investment in research of up to 3%, both at the level of individual member states and the european union as a whole. the prodi commission's proposal was for a spending ceiling of 1.24% of gross national income, but some important countries in the union have already publicly set the ceiling at 1%. such a severe reduction in community financial resources is likely to have a real impact on many of the main policies, including cohesion policy, rural development and the financing of training and research programmes, with which development policies are increasingly closely linked. with such a small budget it would be very foolish to continue talking about the revival of research in europe and more advanced societies.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, on other occasions i have already had the opportunity to point out that a certain method of constructing europe has now reached a point of very severe social and political crisis. however, while the updating of the lisbon strategy is being discussed, concrete choices are being made which may indicate a certain direction. i would like to mention two specific examples. the first example concerns a proposal for a directive, the so-called bolkestein directive, which we believe should be rejected. this is a veritable sum of negative and at the same time messy content, which would lead to a veritable jungle where the fundamental rights of workers and citizens are simultaneously violated and the quality of services is therefore rightly opposed by the trade unions and the wider social forces, and we call for their withdrawal. the second example concerns the reach proposal, which is useful for innovating our chemical industry in terms of quality and health, and we are calling for its rapid adoption. these are two concrete choices that point to two different ideas of europe.

council. mr president, i am pleased that our two institutions reached a global agreement on the budget of the union for next year during the conciliation meeting on 25 november. following your deliberations today, allow me to make a few remarks in this context. as you know, the council had not initially foreseen the use of the flexibility instrument. in our global compromise the council had to accept its use for a substantial amount – eur 185 million – and this was not greeted with enthusiasm by all member states. because of budgetary discipline, the council is of the opinion that choices have to be made, just as, at national level, one cannot simply add up all priorities and expect the bill to be paid. having said that, in preparation for the conciliation meeting, the council listened carefully to parliament's arguments. parliament made it clear from the start that it would not be able to finance its traditional priorities and its new priorities in headings 3 and 4, internal and external policies, under the existing ceilings. with the agreements on the use of the flexibility instrument in heading 3, parliament is in the position to restore the funding for the agencies. at the same time parliament has secured sufficient funding for its priorities. as regards the use of the flexibility instrument in heading 4, i can agree with the draftsman of the opinion of parliament's committee on foreign affairs that the outcome can be seen as quite positive for parliament. i also want to mention the peace process in northern ireland. i am pleased that we were able to agree on the financing of the peace ii programme for 2005-06. we are making a valuable contribution to a long-term solution. finally, the parliament's delegation made it possible to reach agreement by moderating the demands on payment appropriations. we have a balanced result, which the commission says is workable. i look forward to the signing ceremony on thursday.

negotiations on the 2005 budget: a very important issue for parliament and for europe. after fairly tough negotiations on 25 november, when both branches of the budgetary authority showed skill and commission madam president, i would like to tell mr ebner that the 'television without frontiers' directive provides for the principle of separating television advertising and tele-purchasing from the rest of the programme by optical and acoustic means. the same directive states that underground advertising and tele-purchasing are prohibited. as i said in my answer to previous questions, the directive is under review. the public consultation held in 2003 gave the various parties concerned an opportunity to express their views. some operators are in favour of relaxing the advertising rule to take account of technological developments, for example to allow for the sharing of screens and the placing on the market of equipment which would enable viewers to avoid advertising messages. however, in 2003 there was a consensus that advertising should be clearly identified, in order to avoid misleading the public. the same consultation did not identify the development in europe of an advertising practice of product placement which would violate the provisions of the directive. this point was therefore clarified in the commission's 2003 interpretative communication on certain aspects of the provisions on television advertising. i am of course prepared to return to all these issues when i am considering a possible revision of the directive which i intend to present in 2005.
thank you, madam chairman. i am pleased that the commissioner has indicated that she will give priority to consumer protection in this case if there is any new consideration for 2005. the fact is that the practices that have been used so far are not really satisfactory and that there are practices that are not consistent with consumer protection and that need to be remedied.
on behalf of the group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, this report expresses in apodictic terms the pre-emptive rejection of any proposal for the establishment of so-called reception centres in the countries of final transit, which has been put forward by several parties. it is said that their creation would imply difficulties in meeting international human rights obligations, but i do not know what assumptions this is based on. i wonder if the rapporteur has already noted the lack of availability on the part of the leaders of these countries or on the part of the ngos, including european ones, operating in the countries of origin and transit of immigrants. i think that this prejudice is based solely on ideological reasons, since reception centres could certainly play a significant role in meeting both the need for proper controls and the need for humane treatment of migrants. in fact, the refusal to set up such centres leaves the situation unchanged, preventing the appropriate involvement of the transit countries in the management of the migration process. the centre which was recently set up in africa, thanks to the important intervention of my country in the tripoli agreements, has already succeeded, at least so far, in stopping the mediterranean crossings and the tragic shipwrecks of desperate people on the smugglers' carts. its implementation has thus made it possible to achieve a positive result in the fight against the illicit trafficking in human beings.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, as members of parliament we are sitting in this chamber representing the citizens of the 25 nations that make up the european union. the european parliament is the only institution in the union elected by universal suffrage. as the bearers of direct consent and popular mandate, we should be able to strip ourselves of the ideologies of the political forces to which we belong on certain issues. one of these issues should be immigration. history teaches us that the greatest misfortune for a human being is to be uprooted from his place of origin and that immigration arises for a myriad of reasons not all of which are voluntary. an advanced social system - and the union is a collection of advanced states - cannot exclude some aspects of this phenomenon a priori, preferring only the emotional part. the union must safeguard the fundamental principles on which its existence is based, but it must do so for the benefit of all, both its citizens and those of third countries. it must do so by means of a common policy, shared and sustained choices, not ineffective initiatives that isolate a few member states. if immigration policy is to be uniform, it must be accompanied by all its consequences, especially in an age like ours when there are serious concerns about internal security. it is important, among other things, that we do not exclude a priori the organization of an information and preparation system for the migration phenomenon in the countries of origin and transit, an organized and shared system which, when combined with economic aid and incentives in natural areas, could provide positive results for all. the present proposal does not address the problem in this way, nor in a concrete way, and therefore, without the approval of appropriate amendments, it is not acceptable.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, i can identify with the lambert report, because if there is an enemy that prevents europe from growing, it is fear. one way to combat this is to recognize who among those who come to us is worthy of our help. recently, 75 eritreans were arrested by the libyan authorities, subjected to long detention and inhuman treatment. they were then put on a plane to be flown back to their home country. in desperation, they hijacked the plane and forced it to land in sudan. the united nations high commissioner for refugees was able to interview as many as 60 of the 75 eritreans, since the others were imprisoned for the hijacking. all of the people interviewed had the right to asylum, but libya has no law recognizing this right, is not a signatory to the geneva convention, and does not ensure respect for human rights in prisons. on the other hand, countries like my own - italy - do not have a law on the right to asylum, so they reject migrants indiscriminately, without examining their applications and without a fair, fair and individual assessment. even legal immigrants are humiliated and forced to wait years for their residence permits to be renewed. it's clear that these people are considered an enemy at home to be used and discarded. it is europe that must examine asylum applications, while at the same time helping the maghreb countries to move forward on the path of respect for rights. we must ensure that europe, by using the highest standards for the assessment and protection of refugees, turns these flows into legal immigration, which is useful and possible. the european union must therefore create a single european refugee agency and european reception centres to process individual applications and provide legal and linguistic assistance. refugees will be our first allies in promoting economic, civil and democratic growth in their home countries, because the world will only be safer when it stops being so unfair.
the rapporteur. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i believe that the european union's anti-drug strategy, which is on the agenda for our debate, must be based exclusively on a scientific approach, an analysis of the real situation and the experience gained in recent years. on the contrary, the subject is too often, unfortunately, permeated by ideology, dogmatic approaches or emotions. the data available to us allows us to have a real approach. i refer in particular to the data provided to us by the european monitoring centre for drugs, which is based in lisbon. as you know, these data are not only of high scientific value but also of proven reliability. the 2004 lisbon monitoring centre report indicates that the situation regarding heroin deaths and heroin users in europe is broadly stable, but that there is no decrease in hiv infection, which is spreading in some new member states, that cocaine use is increasing in most member states, that cannabis is the most widely used substance, with more than 20% of european citizens having used or using it, and that ecstasy use is equal to, if not higher than, amphetamine use. this shows unequivocally that the strategies adopted so far have not had a positive effect. with regard to drug use in prisons, the observatory indicates that drug users are overrepresented in the prison environment, that inmates' drug use varies from 22% to 86%, depending on the prison population, the prison and the country, and that prisons are a high-risk environment for the spread of infectious diseases. as regards the criminalisation of drug use, the centre explains that in many countries the process of criminalising drug use has led to an increase in the use of these substances. i would point out to my swedish colleagues that the report of the observatory contains a specific reference to the situation in sweden, where it cites a study which shows that the repressive strategies implemented in that country have intensified the drug market and where it concludes that there is no precise evidence that the criminalisation of drug use has a deterrent effect on young people. finally, as regards drug trafficking, europol indicates that drug trafficking represents approximately 2 to 5 per cent of world gdp, which goes directly into the hands of the drug traffickers and the major criminal organizations on the planet. the u.s. drug enforcement administration estimates that afghanistan will export over five thousand tons of opium in 2005, 95 percent of which will go to europe. this shows that the action plan approved by the un in 1998 has not produced positive effects, also in view of the importance of drug trafficking for the financing of mafias and terrorism. a prominent prosecutor like d'ambrosio explained to us that in order to finance the madrid attack, al qaeda terrorists traded tritol for 250 kilograms of hashish. our report, which was approved by the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, is therefore motivated by the need to strengthen the fight against drug trafficking, the mafia and terrorism and to give priority to the protection of life and health. the question cannot be dealt with as a matter of public policy or as a matter of repression, but only as a social and economic issue. for these reasons, we have focused our report on the harm reduction strategy and substitution treatment in prisons, and on the need to apply scientific knowledge to the effects produced by drugs, to initiate scientific studies on the costs and benefits of current drug control policies, to help producing countries to convert agriculture, to study the effects that cannabis, opium and coca leaves can have in the medical field and, finally, to intervene in the confiscation of assets from drug laundering and trafficking by mafiosi and terrorists. i therefore call on the european parliament to approve this report, on the commission to support it and on the council - which is unfortunately absent, having obviously already taken decisions - to consider implementing this recommendation at its meeting in three days' time. i would also point out that this recommendation was drawn up with the support of a number of organisations from below.
on behalf of the group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, prohibitionism in the world has always produced two results: on the one hand, there is the increase in deaths and incarcerations among drug users, who are among the prison population with the highest recidivism rate, and on the other hand, unlimited big business for all kinds of drug traffickers. on the contrary, this project aims primarily at protecting the health of each person. that's why harm reduction strategies are absolutely critical. only by keeping alive people who cannot or will not stop using drugs at the moment can we hope that in the future they will be able to break free from all forms of addiction. we must not forget that the majority of deaths are due to aids and heart disease, that is to say, pathological conditions linked to the way in which they are consumed. this is why damage reduction involves low-threshold interventions in the streets and centres. we must also make a firm distinction between drug dealers and drug users, who are often lumped together by repressive policies. finally, complementarities between social interventions and interventions through pharmacological substitutes are essential. we must avoid any moralism that pits them against each other.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i hope that parliament will approve the eurlings report on turkey as approved by the committee on foreign affairs, and i hope that it will do so by secret ballot. i think that the members are mistaken in thinking that a secret ballot could help to change that direction, and i expect that members in this house will be able to show that they are not under any pressure in the exercise of their free mandate. as regards the rest of external relations, i would like to express our agreement with all the measures that anticipate constitutional reforms, and i agree with many of the points made by mr nicolaï. we have seen a sign of strengthening the initiative in this direction in the high representative's activism in ukraine. however, this initiative should be supported by more instruments, which are provided for in the constitution but which perhaps can be anticipated. we are also very concerned about the situation in the middle east. however, we point out the risk that measures aimed at creating a free trade area will prevail over other policies. i would like to stress that we do not want to create a zone of liberalization alone, and we must be careful not to weaken the regional and subregional dimension of this policy. as regards human rights and democracy, i recommend that a genuine convention be negotiated with the neighbouring countries. the european union is not only a political union, but also a political union of the peoples of europe. on the middle east, we must ensure that the palestinian presidential elections give a new impetus to the road map and that the unilateral decisions of the israeli government, such as the withdrawal from gaza, are included in the negotiations with the quartet, with the aim of arriving at the establishment of the palestinian state in a definite time. to this end, it would be necessary to put this issue at the heart of relations between the european union and the united states. as regards iraq, we believe that the desired change has not occurred even after the sharm el-sheikh conference and that the objective of the elections is in danger of being compromised by the situation of violence in the country. in conclusion, i would ask the council and the commission to also look at the situation in cambodia, where there is a humanitarian emergency affecting children.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the next european council is of particular importance because, as the representatives of the two institutions have said, it will inevitably deal with particularly important subjects, such as the enlargement of the union to bulgaria and romania, the possible opening of negotiations with croatia and turkey, the review of progress made in the fight against terrorism and, in particular, the integration of an information capacity within the general secretariat of the council and the preparation of the financial framework for the period 2007-13. we have already expressed on several occasions the desirability of the accession of bulgaria and romania, although we stress with particular concern the areas in which there are still great distances between the reality of these two countries and that of the european average. in this connection, we would like to recall the legislation on human rights, social protection of workers and economic development, which should reach substantial levels by the end of the decade. while we are in principle in favour of opening negotiations with turkey, which, i repeat, is quite different from accession, we cannot forget the need for the recognition of the armenian genocide and for a genuine solution to the cyprus problem. it is incomprehensible to the common sense that a state which aspires to join the union can militarily occupy part of another state which is a member. we are not at all concerned about the voting system we will have to face. i believe in the freedom of every member to decide, regardless of whether the vote is open or secret. i think that this is a purely formal matter and certainly not a substantive one. the fight against terrorism is closely linked to the development of the middle east peace process and to the situation in certain countries where conflicts are not yet over. i believe, however, that the union must be increasingly present with its policy and its aid in those parts of the world around it where conflict and sometimes tragic events are taking place, in order to exert a balancing influence in support of the forces which accept the values which characterize our democracy.
mr president, i don't mean to steal your job or give you advice. however, even if a request for a roll-call vote has been made, under rule 162 (3) of the rules of procedure the request for a secret ballot takes precedence over the request for a roll-call vote.
mr president, i am very pleased to announce a statement of vote on the report on turkey's accession to the european union. i am aware that i voted against turkey's accession to the european union, but i would like to explain how i came to this conclusion. on 9 december last, when i was in reggio calabria, invited by the representative of the pensioners' party, carlo rositani, to give a lecture at the tommaso gulli school, i asked a class of about 120 girls aged between 16 and 19 to vote for or against turkey's accession to the european union. the result of the vote by these 120 young people was as follows: one hundred out of 120 girls voted against membership, while the other 20 abstained. i therefore gladly followed the young people's vote.
mr president, i oppose the european parliament's decision to open accession negotiations with turkey, because turkey is not europe! moreover, there is a danger that one day we will see a flag - the turkish flag - waving in these institutions, a flag which is still stained with the blood of the armenian people, whose holocaust is not recognized by turkey. we have betrayed the spirit of lepanto, a thousand-year-old tradition of a europe which is and wants to remain attached to its christian roots. the turkish society is still deeply rooted in the principles of islamic fundamentalism and this is a serious danger that europe should take more seriously. we are therefore proud of our vote against the opening of the procedures for turkey's accession to europe and we consider it shameful that part of european politics which has irresponsibly supported this decision which goes against the will of the majority of the citizens. we call for a referendum to be held in all the countries of the european union to give the people a voice, because the people of europe are opposed to turkey's accession.
in writing. the delegation from the alliance nationale voted in favour of the eurlings report on the opening of the accession procedure for turkey, a procedure which will take several years and whose opening is unequivocally a prelude to a change in the turkish government's policy not only with regard to the acquis communautaire and the copenhagen clauses but especially with regard to the solution of the armenian and kurdish questions. the national alliance believes that the council decision of 17 december should make it clear that the opening of negotiations with turkey means the recognition by the turkish government of the state of cyprus as a full member of the european union and that, consequently, the solution of the cyprus problem, namely the withdrawal of the 35 000 turkish troops occupying cyprus, means that they occupy a territory of the european union. the european union will ensure that the guarantees and clauses contained in the report are respected, without which it will not be possible to reach a favourable conclusion to turkey's accession process.
in writing. turkey's eventual accession to the european union brings with it many benefits and some risks. the first are more political than economic, since turkey is not only a member of nato but also has a customs union treaty with the eu which already allows free trade in goods. its accession, in addition to fulfilling the dream of millions of citizens to be part of the full range of the secular western world, would rebalance the centre of political and economic influence by moving towards the mediterranean basin, accrediting europe as a world power and not closed within its geopolitical borders. the risks to be taken into account are primarily linked to the "dilution" of the possibility of seeing the birth of a europe with its own common foreign policy and the possible difficulty of making two different religious and cultural identities live together. can a democratic state be defined as respectful of ethnic minorities? secondly, it would have a negative impact on the allocation of european funds to regions currently considered 'lagging behind in development', such as sicily, at least in the short term. finally, turkey should withdraw from the part of cyprus that is militarily occupied, on the understanding that it will have to be willing to face up to history by acknowledging the mistakes made over the last hundred years... mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the new italian socialist party wishes mr basescu well in his new job. the many young romanians who have supported him in recent months and who are filling the squares and streets of bucharest and the rest of the country with celebrations, have placed in him, beyond political affiliation, the hope of change and a different, new and better future. basescu has a difficult and exciting task: the european union is committed to the development of the country and to the consolidation of its democratic institutions. we hope - indeed we are certain - that the starting point of this process will be the full integration of romania into the political, economic and social bloc of the european union. in the two years that remain, as we look back with satisfaction on the achievements we have made, it will be our task to ensure that the many situations which are still far from european standards are improved in a concrete, real and significant way. i am referring in particular to the necessary and urgent qualitative leap forward in the field of human rights, with particular reference to the demands of the roma minority, which is often discriminated against with the consent of the government authorities. it is a question of two and a half million citizens living on the margins of society in absolutely unacceptable conditions. i am referring again to respect for the demands and aspirations of the 1.5 million hungarian minority, the treatment of the disabled, the inadequate conditions in health facilities, especially those for patients with mental problems, the violent methods and the actual physical abuse perpetrated by the police, a justice system far removed from efficiency and independence criteria, and information which is not yet fully free. visiting the country, i was struck and disturbed by the many, many children wandering the streets alone. we must demand a concrete commitment from the government on this issue. it is a legal and moral obligation, not an option. we must therefore do everything possible to guarantee to every child in romania, as in europe and the rest of the world, the right to a peaceful and happy childhood and the right to a smile of hope in life.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i join the many positive remarks made about the balance of the report by mr moscovici, which, while not concealing any of the critical points of romania's process of rapprochement with the european union, has clearly shown appreciation for the considerable efforts and results, albeit still partial, achieved by romania in this process. this morning, with great ease, this parliament started the process of turkey's accession to the european union. we are in favour of romania's accession for exactly the same reasons as those which led us to express our firm opposition to turkey's accession. romania is europe, romania has a civil and political society based on values which, despite the undeniable ambiguities, must be considered to be perfectly comparable to the values on which the european union is founded. certain areas in which fundamental steps still need to be taken, such as the multiannual strategy against crime, the question of state aid, which is particularly close to the heart of us liberals, the delicate question of the restitution of property and churches, and the modernisation of a judicial system which does not seem compatible with a society such as the european one, which is characterized by systems which are not immune to the direct influence and dominance of the state, certainly deserve encouragement and support. i heard a member of a political party who voted in favour of turkey's accession, who took up the strong remarks made by the ngos on the respect for human rights in romania. i would have liked to have heard the same remarks about turkey, which would have been much more justified. however, it is absolutely necessary to remind the romanian authorities of the urgent issue of illegal immigration. a colleague spoke of the minors, who rightly struck a chord with him during a visit to romania. i do not need to go to romania, because all i have to do is walk the streets of turin, milan, genoa and rome to see romanian minors being exploited by criminal organizations. we must say no more to illegal immigration and romania must defend its external borders and cooperate actively to stamp out this phenomenon, which does not honour the civilization of ionesco, mircea eliade and so many other great authors and thinkers of a civilization which we feel is close and common.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i thank the rapporteur, mr moscovici, for presenting us with a balanced report, which highlights the progress made by romania in recent years. baroness nicholson was right to recall the basis on which romania started and the reasons which made the path more difficult for this country. however, mr moscovici also highlighted the limitations and shortcomings of the romanian situation today. some colleagues from the countries which joined the union on 1 may are calling for greater severity. i would like to see us re-read what was said in this house when, before these countries joined, we were looking at the situation they had reached on their way to the union. it is very likely that the shortcomings were considerable then as well, but it was nevertheless considered appropriate to allow their accession within the time limits. we also have a safeguard clause for romania, which was not provided for in the other cases. i therefore believe that it is right to give this country the chance to play out all its capacities over the next two years so that it can be prepared when it actually joins our union. there is no doubt that there are issues which concern our fellow citizens, first and foremost the inadequate fight against organised crime. if we ask the citizens on the streets of our countries to express the fear they often have of criminal gangs, we will certainly find the will to encourage the romanian government to continue in this direction. as mr leinen rightly pointed out, the fight against corruption is very widespread in the countries entering our union. i would like to return to the subject of children. this is a subject i have dealt with extensively in my years with baroness nicholson. child protection should be at the heart of our thinking. i believe, however, that the proposal made by the rapporteur of an international committee is a good solution. if we want to prevent these children from spending their adolescence and youth on the streets of our cities or in institutions, we must take account of the dozens of families who are already in contact with these children and who are eager to give them affection. we therefore welcome the proposal made by mr moscovici to that effect. finally, mr president, we must consider the important value of stability. we shall see whether the result of the recent elections in bucharest will lead to political stability. i believe that is what we all want, because only with stability can there be any hope of seeing romania ready for its appointments.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i thank mr moscovici for his excellent report. the accession of romania and bulgaria marks the completion of the fifth enlargement round, which will bring about a larger and more democratic europe, which will emerge on the global political scene, full of great possibilities and deep concerns, with its distinctive feature of civilised europe. europe is therefore not just an area of free markets and free movement with a single currency, but above all the capacity and willingness to export peacefully the model of the rule of law, the protection of minorities and human rights, sustainable development, and an efficient and cohesive economy and finance. this is what the spirit and the letter of the constitutional treaty and the charter of nice say. we therefore welcome the successes and the progressive improvement that romania has achieved in various areas, as mr moscovici has pointed out, in accordance with the copenhagen criteria, including the strengthening of respect for minorities, both hungarian and roma. however, romania still needs to make rapid progress in fulfilling all the commitments arising from accession, especially in the area of the acquis communautaire. structural interventions are not enough to combat poverty. corruption must also be combated by preventive measures and all laws must be respected. finally, we welcome the improvement in legislation on child protection and intercountry adoption. in the latter case, i would urge the romanian authorities to resolve promptly the problem of moratorium-affected adoptions, which are depriving children of a family to which they are entitled. in the name of this universal right, which is one of the strengths of the constitutional treaty, and in addition to the creation of an international commission to act immediately, i ask the newly elected romanian authorities not to waste precious time and i invite them to give these children, on christmas, parents who already love them and with whom they can build a better future in a europe of peace.
on behalf of the group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of our group i would like to welcome the commissioner. let me get straight to the point. this evening parliament is being asked to approve the extension of the specific programme on human rights and democracy for two years. in principle, our group has no objections. however, in these months of dialogue with the commission we have made our agreement subject to certain political conditions, namely a clear signal from the commission which you represent that it is willing to engage in a structured dialogue with parliament. it is not a question of claiming management tasks which are not within the responsibilities of this parliament, but of claiming a capacity for political evaluation of this measure and, more generally, of all initiatives concerning the development of democracy and the commitment to the issue of human rights. we ask for an ex ante assessment of the priorities of the interventions and an ex post assessment of the quality of the interventions and the extent to which they have succeeded in making a real advance in democracy. in other words, commissioner, this parliament is asking you to be involved in defining the guidelines for our activities and in assessing the adequacy of the results we have achieved. in these years many questions have remained unanswered and much information has not been received or has been provided in a very summary manner. we do not know how much money has actually been spent through this programme, what projects have been completed, where they have been carried out and with what concrete results, why funds are blocked in some countries, or what is being done to unblock these funds. we do not want to break the sacred rules of comitology, but we must find ways of establishing a relationship which is not based on a mere verbal expression of goodwill. commissioner, we appreciate your offer of cooperation and our willingness to extend the duration of this programme must be interpreted as an act of trust. it is important to make it clear that this parliament cannot be regarded, as it often is, as a notary called upon to formally approve expenditure but must be limited to that. that is not in the interests of this parliament or the community institutions. we believe that this programme should be streamlined and speeded up as much as possible, that local non-governmental organisations should be given more responsibility and that special emphasis should be placed on micro-projects and campaigns which have in the past been used in the difficult democratic environment of certain countries. we must work a little more on democracy, because we have neglected it so far, working mainly in the field of human rights. of course, we must also build up a capacity for dialogue, for which, madam commissioner, this parliament will give you our confidence and our vote. we hope, however, that from tomorrow this will be translated into a daily dialogue, not only formal but also substantive, in the examination of the choices that will be made and in the definition of the objectives that we will set ourselves.

on behalf of the group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, madam commissioner, i would like to state at once that i am in favour of extending the community action programme on civil protection for two years. it seems to me that rather than a free choice, it is a forced solution with no alternative. we have reached the end of the current programme without a lasting and concrete solution to the problem. this shows how marginal the issue of civil protection is in europe. i believe i have gained some modest experience in this field, as chairman of the civil protection committee of the province of catania, the most exposed in italy to seismic and volcanic risk, but also having coordinated, on behalf of the italian government, the emergency during the dramatic eruption of mount etna in the summer of 2001. in these years i have been able to consolidate the idea that civil protection, in addition to being a pleasant topic for round tables or pleasant social evenings, is a matter inextricably linked to the security of citizens and security, as is well known, before being a constitutional right, is a physiological right. i think i can say that the european union is lagging behind in this area, very seriously, scandalously. the matter was tackled only five years ago with a series of initiatives which - according to the rapporteur himself, whose work i appreciated - sound good in theory but in practice have serious shortcomings. in fact, the approach seems schizophrenic not only in terms of logistics but also in terms of the lack of financial coverage. commissioner, what results have the information monitoring centre, the solidarity fund and the community mechanism produced in terms of risk forecasting and prevention, as well as emergency and post-emergency management? what qualitative results have they produced in the european union? is it possible to know the effects? is it possible to have a detailed report? the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. how can civil protection in europe be allowed to remain divided into competencies spread over the environment, health, land, agriculture and internal policies? it is necessary to set up a european agency for civil protection, as i took the liberty of asking the commission two years ago. in addition to the clear involvement of an operational structure, especially in relation to the member states least sensitive to the problem, this solution would have the advantage of developing and standardizing procedures and methodologies for the analysis and mitigation of natural and technological risks, overcoming the criterion of territoriality, and of creating a real task force. in conclusion, i believe that it is necessary to promote and support, through an organised training activity and with equipment and means, the action of volunteers in civil protection, which becomes an indispensable resource in dealing with emergencies and natural and other disasters. i hope that the commission will find the strength, the will and the courage to confront parliament on this matter.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, mr ribeiro's excellent report on the proposal for a council regulation on the protection of deep-sea coral reefs from the effects of demersal fishing, more generally known as 'trawl fishing', is timely and supported by numerous studies in this field. the impact on these environments - as indeed happens for all types of marine environment affected by undifferentiated sampling techniques - affects, as is well known, not only pelagic and edible sessile species, but also non-edible sessile forms and, more generally, causes serious damage to environments essential for reproduction. this leads to the depletion of the resource and, consequently, of the economic potential, with the greatest negative impact on local communities, in particular on small craft fisheries. the protection of these environments, in addition to the recognition of the international conventions cited in the report, is therefore an important measure to ensure not only environmental protection but also the continuity of social and economic prospects for the human communities that persist in the regions concerned. this is generally true of all regions where fishing is a traditional resource which, as such, must be safeguarded, not only by limiting industrial fishing by fleets from other countries, but also by ensuring the continuity of breeding habitats. the involvement of local communities, in particular those working in the sector, is essential to this end. i therefore think it desirable that the institutions, local authorities and trade associations, with the help of the community institutions, should, in addition to monitoring protected areas, take care of the periodic scientific monitoring of habitats, as well as environmentally friendly information and the professionalism of the staff. it is also necessary to encourage the potential of small artisanal enterprises by providing them with modern tools for research, identification and harvesting of resources, in order to promote greater selectivity in the harvesting of marine resources and to enable these enterprises to survive competition from industrial fishing fleets, particularly those of japan and the united states.
i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. i'll be very brief. i wanted to say that i voted for the report and i hope that we can make considerable progress in this area in the future. in this context, i also asked the new commission to propose that a civil service be introduced for young people in the european union, whether male or female, in order to create the conditions, especially in such situations, so that in the event of disasters, but also in solidarity service, there is more responsibility and more awareness among the population within the european union. i am, as i said, in favour of this report and i am glad that it is being pursued in this direction.
